+  # Phase 10 REST services and worker (modules/services; owner of the values: service-engineer).
+  # Every value is PROVISIONAL unless a ruling is named. Every integer must be >= 1. The synchronous
+  # deadline is operations.synchronous-deadline-ms; the question length limit is

+  services {
+    # Ruling 10: answers use no language model (rule planner, template synthesis) unless this is
+    # false. Live model use is an explicit opt-in, as --live is for the CLIs. /v1/explain reports it.
+    stub-llm = true                       # SCHOLARCHAIN_STUB_LLM
+    metrics-enabled = true                # GET /metrics on every service (ruling 1)
+    # Ruling 5: NAME of the environment variable holding the API key (ECS injects it from Secrets
+    # Manager). Never the key. The loader never reads this variable into configuration.
+    api-key-env = "SCHOLARCHAIN_API_KEY"
+    # Request body cap (ruling 6): a 2000-char question JSON-escaped at 6 bytes per char (12000) plus
+    # 3 x 5 filter terms of 200 chars (18000) is about 30 KiB; 64 KiB leaves twice that.
+    max-body-bytes = 65536
+    # Requests waiting for a thread, per service; beyond it the service answers 503.
+    max-queued-requests = 64
+
+    query-api {
+      port = 8080
+      # A sync query holds a thread up to the deadline, and the model throttle (8 per minute)
+      # serializes model calls anyway, so more threads only wait longer.
+      threads = 8
+      # Accepted POST /v1/query per minute per instance (ruling 6, 429): at 2 to 4 model calls per
+      # question and 8 calls per minute, one instance answers 2 to 4 live questions per minute.
+      query-rate-per-minute = 10
+    }
+    graph-evidence-api {
+      port = 8081
+      threads = 16                        # bounded store reads only, no model calls
+    }
+    worker {
+      port = 8082                         # health, readiness, and metrics only
+      threads = 2
+      concurrency = 1                     # jobs in flight per worker; model quota (ruling 10)
+      poll-wait-seconds = 20              # SQS long poll, 1 to 20
+      # Must exceed runtime.question.deadline-ms + runtime.llm.timeout-ms (180 s + 30 s; the
+      # deadline is cooperative) so a running job's message never reappears; at most 43200 (SQS).
+      visibility-timeout-seconds = 300
+    }
+    # The job queue and its dead-letter queue are created by Terraform (Phase 12). The redrive
+    # policy there must use the same max-receive-count (1 to 1000).
+    queue {
+      url = "https://sqs.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/000000000000/example-scholarchain-jobs"  # fake; SCHOLARCHAIN_JOB_QUEUE_URL
+      dead-letter-url = "https://sqs.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/000000000000/example-scholarchain-jobs-dlq"  # fake; SCHOLARCHAIN_JOB_DLQ_URL
+      max-receive-count = 3               # SCHOLARCHAIN_JOB_MAX_RECEIVE_COUNT
+    }
+    # DynamoDB TTL (attribute expiresAt, ruling 11). idempotency-hours <= job-hours <=
+    # runtime.audit.ttl-days x 24, so a stored reference never outlives what it points to.
+    ttl {
+      idempotency-hours = 24
+      job-hours = 168
+    }
+    evidence-max-content-chars = 2000     # GET /v1/evidence text cap (ruling 8)
+    # Ruling 9: reads only, retryable errors only. Same meaning as the storage RetryPolicy
+    # (attempts include the first; delay before retry n = min(max-delay-ms, base-delay-ms x 2^(n-1))).
+    # One circuit breaker per downstream opens after breaker-failure-threshold consecutive failures
+    # and fails fast for breaker-open-ms.

+    }

 object EdgeType:
   given ReadWriter[EdgeType] = enumRW(values, _.toString)
+
+/** State of an async query job (Phase 10 ruling 7). Writers move a job only along
+  * [[JobState.canMoveTo]], with conditional writes, so a redelivered message never runs a job
+  * twice. SUCCEEDED and FAILED are final.
+  */
+enum JobState:
+  case QUEUED, RUNNING, SUCCEEDED, FAILED
+
+  def isFinal: Boolean = this == SUCCEEDED || this == FAILED
+
+  /** QUEUED -> RUNNING -> SUCCEEDED or FAILED; QUEUED -> FAILED ends a job that never ran (for
+    * example a poison message). Nothing else, and no state moves to itself.
+    */
+  def canMoveTo(next: JobState): Boolean = (this, next) match
+    case (QUEUED, RUNNING) | (QUEUED, FAILED)     => true
+    case (RUNNING, SUCCEEDED) | (RUNNING, FAILED) => true
+    case _                                        => false
+object JobState:
+  given ReadWriter[JobState] = enumRW(values, _.toString)
+
+/** Typed error codes of the REST error model (spec "Error model", Phase 10 ruling 6), each with its
+  * HTTP status and default retryable flag. Also the error of a FAILED job. The wire form is the
+  * case name. Appending a code is compatible; renaming or removing one breaks stored FAILED jobs.
+  * FORBIDDEN (403) is reserved and unused in the single-key deployment (ruling 5).
+  */
+enum ErrorCode(val httpStatus: Int, val retryable: Boolean):
+  case MALFORMED_JSON extends ErrorCode(400, false)
+  case INVALID_FIELD extends ErrorCode(400, false)
+  case REQUEST_TOO_LARGE extends ErrorCode(400, false)
+  case UNAUTHORIZED extends ErrorCode(401, false)
+  case FORBIDDEN extends ErrorCode(403, false)
+  case ROUTE_NOT_FOUND extends ErrorCode(404, false)
+  case QUERY_NOT_FOUND extends ErrorCode(404, false)
+  case JOB_NOT_FOUND extends ErrorCode(404, false)
+  case NODE_NOT_FOUND extends ErrorCode(404, false)
+  case EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND extends ErrorCode(404, false)
+  case IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT extends ErrorCode(409, false)
+  case JOB_NOT_FINISHED extends ErrorCode(409, true) // poll again later
+  case UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRAINT extends ErrorCode(422, false)
+  case UNSUPPORTED_RETRIEVAL_MODE extends ErrorCode(422, false)
+  case RATE_LIMITED extends ErrorCode(429, true)
+  case DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE extends ErrorCode(502, true)
+  case NOT_READY extends ErrorCode(503, true)
+  case CIRCUIT_OPEN extends ErrorCode(503, true)
+  case OVERLOADED extends ErrorCode(503, true)
+  case DEADLINE_EXCEEDED extends ErrorCode(504, true)
+  case DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT extends ErrorCode(504, true)
+  case INTERNAL_ERROR extends ErrorCode(500, false)
+object ErrorCode:
+  given ReadWriter[ErrorCode] = enumRW(values, _.toString)

+  /** The `services.*` rules (Phase 10 rulings 5 to 11). */
+  private def validateServices(c: ScholarChainConfig): List[String] =
+    def check(ok: Boolean, msg: String) = if ok then Nil else List(msg)
+    val s = c.services
+    val ports = Seq(s.queryApi.port, s.graphEvidenceApi.port, s.worker.port)
+    // The worker runs each job under runtime.question.deadline-ms; the deadline is cooperative, so a
+    // running model attempt can overrun it by up to runtime.llm.timeout-ms.
+    val workerBoundMs = c.runtime.question.deadlineMs.toLong + c.runtime.llm.timeoutMs

+      ) ++

+  test("Phase 10 job ID: frozen value, one job per query, shape, and guard") {
+    val q = "qry-e605c59cdcfda782444ceae12233ca77"
+    val j = StableIds.jobId(q)
+    // Computed independently: sha256("job" U+001F q), first 32 hex digits.
+    assert(j == "job-4adb9f615f3369c2e77c2dac2d758ec4")
+    assert(StableIds.jobId(q) == j)
+    assert(StableIds.jobId("qry-" + "0" * 32) != j)
+    assert(IdFormat.isJobId(j) && !IdFormat.isQueryId(j) && !IdFormat.isJobId(q))
+    assert(!IdFormat.isJobId("job-01J") && !IdFormat.isJobId("job-" + "A" * 32))
+    assertThrows[IllegalArgumentException](StableIds.jobId("job-01J"))
+  }
+
+  test("Phase 10 node ID: every GraphNode ID shape is a node ID; other text is not") {
+    for id <- Seq(researcherA, workId, mention1.mentionId, profileChunkId, "ror:03yrm5c26") do
+      assert(IdFormat.isNodeId(id), id)
+    assert(
+      IdFormat.isNodeId(StableIds.communityId("graph-v1", "all-years", 2000, 2026, researcherA))
+    )
+    for bad <- Seq("", "Researcher", "a b:c", "qry-e605c59cdcfda782444ceae12233ca77", "../etc") do
+      assert(!IdFormat.isNodeId(bad), bad)
+  }

+  test("Phase 10 JobState: frozen names, wire form, and the only allowed transitions") {
+    import JobState.*
+    assert(
+      JobState.values.map(_.toString).toVector == Vector("QUEUED", "RUNNING", "SUCCEEDED", "FAILED")
+    )
+    assert(upickle.default.write(RUNNING) == "\"RUNNING\"")
+    assert(upickle.default.read[JobState]("\"SUCCEEDED\"") == SUCCEEDED)
+    assert(scala.util.Try(upickle.default.read[JobState]("\"DONE\"")).isFailure)
+    val allowed = for a <- JobState.values; b <- JobState.values if a.canMoveTo(b) yield (a, b)
+    assert(
+      allowed.toSet == Set(
+        QUEUED -> RUNNING,
+        QUEUED -> FAILED,
+        RUNNING -> SUCCEEDED,
+        RUNNING -> FAILED
+      )
+    )
+    assert(JobState.values.filter(_.isFinal).toSet == Set(SUCCEEDED, FAILED))
+  }

+  test("Phase 10 ErrorCode: frozen names, HTTP statuses (ruling 6), and retryable defaults") {
+    // Appending a code is compatible; renaming or removing one breaks stored FAILED jobs.
+    val expected = Vector(
+      ("MALFORMED_JSON", 400, false),
+      ("INVALID_FIELD", 400, false),
+      ("REQUEST_TOO_LARGE", 400, false),
+      ("UNAUTHORIZED", 401, false),
+      ("FORBIDDEN", 403, false),
+      ("ROUTE_NOT_FOUND", 404, false),
+      ("QUERY_NOT_FOUND", 404, false),
+      ("JOB_NOT_FOUND", 404, false),
+      ("NODE_NOT_FOUND", 404, false),
+      ("EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND", 404, false),
+      ("IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT", 409, false),
+      ("JOB_NOT_FINISHED", 409, true),
+      ("UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRAINT", 422, false),
+      ("UNSUPPORTED_RETRIEVAL_MODE", 422, false),
+      ("RATE_LIMITED", 429, true),
+      ("DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE", 502, true),
+      ("NOT_READY", 503, true),
+      ("CIRCUIT_OPEN", 503, true),
+      ("OVERLOADED", 503, true),
+      ("DEADLINE_EXCEEDED", 504, true),
+      ("DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT", 504, true),
+      ("INTERNAL_ERROR", 500, false)
+    )
+    assert(ErrorCode.values.map(e => (e.toString, e.httpStatus, e.retryable)).toVector == expected)
+    // Every status of the spec error model has a code.
+    assert(
+      ErrorCode.values.map(_.httpStatus).toSet ==
+        Set(400, 401, 403, 404, 409, 422, 429, 502, 503, 504, 500)
+    )
+    assert(upickle.default.write(ErrorCode.CIRCUIT_OPEN) == "\"CIRCUIT_OPEN\"")
+    assert(upickle.default.read[ErrorCode]("\"RATE_LIMITED\"") == ErrorCode.RATE_LIMITED)
+    assert(scala.util.Try(upickle.default.read[ErrorCode]("\"TEAPOT\"")).isFailure)
+  }

-**Numbers (ruling 17).** Text is first stripped of every bundle text with a digit, space, or capital (names, titles, IDs, versions), so digits inside them are not numbers; window names are kept for the window rule. A year range (`2000-2014`, `2000 to 2014`) is a window mention unless both years are year facts the claim asserts (first and last collaboration years, hop years, work years), which are facts, not windows. Every other number must be bound: some values entry of the claim passed `asserted-values`, has a numeric fact name, and a cited item's stored value of that fact equals the written number. Integers compare exactly; a decimal passes when equal, or when at least 2 decimals are written and the stored value rounded half-up to them equals it (0.55 never verifies as 1 or 0.6). The sign of a stored change is not required in the text. Window keys (`window`, `*WindowStartYear`, `*WindowEndYear`) never bind a number: a window bound is not a free number. The bound fact's measure must be named: for scalar facts the measure name nearest to the number in its clause (`;`, `:`, a sentence end; parenthetical remarks that do not contain the number are skipped) must be one of the fact's names (`Verifier.Families`, for example "distinct coauthors" or "breadth" for `distinctCoauthorCount`, "publications", "papers", or "works" for `publicationCount`, "weighted degree" or "intensity" for `weightedDegree`); list facts (`rank.<k>.value`, `entry.<k>.count`, `CHANGE#` from, to, and delta facts) need the measure named once in the text; year facts need no name. An asserted `edgeExists` of `false` binds the number 0 as a joint publication count (the pair has no edge in that window).
+**Numbers (ruling 17).** Text is first stripped of every bundle text with a digit, space, or capital (names, titles, IDs, versions), so digits inside them are not numbers; window names are kept for the window rule. A year range (`2000-2014`, `2000 to 2014`) is a window mention unless both years are year facts the claim asserts (first and last collaboration years, hop years, work years), which are facts, not windows. Every other number must be bound: some values entry of the claim passed `asserted-values`, has a numeric fact name, and a cited item's stored value of that fact equals the written number. Integers compare exactly; a decimal passes when equal, or when at least 2 decimals are written and the stored value rounded half-up to them equals it (0.55 never verifies as 1 or 0.6). The sign of a stored change is not required in the text. Window keys (`window`, `*WindowStartYear`, `*WindowEndYear`) never bind a number: a window bound is not a free number. The bound fact's measure must be named: for scalar facts the measure name nearest to the number in its clause (`;`, `:`, a sentence end; parenthetical remarks that do not contain the number are skipped) must be one of the fact's names (`Verifier.Families`, for example "distinct coauthors" or "breadth" for `distinctCoauthorCount`, "publications", "papers", or "works" for `publicationCount`, "weighted degree", "intensity", or "joint publications" for `weightedDegree`, as synthesis-v2 and v3 define intensity; "average", "mean", "per paper", or "coauthors per paper" for `averageCoauthorsPerPaper`, review 3 n6); list facts (`rank.<k>.value`, `entry.<k>.count`, `CHANGE#` from, to, and delta facts) need the measure named once in the text; year facts need no name. An asserted `edgeExists` of `false` binds the number 0 as a joint publication count (the pair has no edge in that window).
+
+**Digit groups and corpus measures (verifier-v5, Phase 10 work order 9).** Before number words are converted, digit-group commas are removed (`Verifier.ungroup`): one to three digits followed by groups of exactly three digits, as in "141,367" (live B18, where the claim was removed because 141 and 367 were read as two numbers). "2001,2002", "1,23", and "12,3456" stay apart. The corpus window-summary facts have their measure names: `researchers` ("researchers", "nodes"), `components` ("components", "connected components"), `largestComponentSize` and `largestCommunitySize` (the component and community families), `largestComponentFraction` and `largestCommunityFraction` ("share", "fraction", "proportion", "percent"), `singletonResearchers` and `singletonCommunities` ("singleton(s)", "isolated"), `communities` and `communitiesAtLeastMinSize` ("community", "communities"), `communityMinSize` ("size", "members", "or more", "at least"), `communityRounds` ("rounds", "iterations"), `coauthorEdges` (the edges family), and `years` ("years"); their `from`, `to`, and `delta` forms are list facts named once in the text. A percentage is not converted: "69.4%" never binds a stored 0.694.
 
 **Number words (ruling 29, N1; `Verifier.wordsToDigits`).** After the bundle texts are stripped and year ranges are set aside, number words are rewritten as digits, so they are bound exactly like digits in claims, summary lines, uncertainty lines, the fixed notes (their numbers stay free numbers for uncertainty lines), and the `context-only` rule. Case is ignored. In order:
 - zero expressions attached to a relation or measure become 0: "never" or "not" (an optional adverb in "-ly" between) before "coauthored", "coauthor", "collaborated", "collaborate", "co-wrote", "co-write", or "published", "worked", "written" + "together" (becomes "0 joint"); "no" before "coauthorship" or "collaboration(s)", optionally followed by "edge", "link", "tie", "connection", or "relationship" (becomes "0 joint"), but not when "path" follows; "no" before optional adjectives (direct, joint, shared, common, distinct, unique, other, further, recorded, stored, known) and then "coauthor(s)", "collaborator(s)", "publication(s)", "paper(s)", "work(s)", or "article(s)" (becomes "0 <rest>"); "none" (becomes 0). "No one" is not a zero;
 - "a single" and "once" are 1, "twice" is 2;
-- ordinals "first" to "tenth" become "1st" to "10th" (the rank measure), except when a year (19xx or 20xx) follows within four words, as in "first in 2001" or "their first joint publication was in 2001", which is a time marker; a year range has already been set aside, so "ranks first in 2015-2026" is still a rank;
+- ordinals "first" to "nineteenth", "twentieth" to "ninetieth", and tens-unit compounds ("twenty-first", "forty-ninth") become "1st" to "99th" (rankings hold the top 50, so every stored rank can be checked; review 3 n5). Only "first" can be a time marker: "first" followed within four words by a year (19xx or 20xx), in a clause (`;`, `:`, `!`, `?`, or a sentence end) with no rank word ("rank..." , "place", "placed", "position..." or "#"), as in "first in 2001", "their first joint publication was in 2001", or "first collaborated in 2009-2012" (number words are now converted before year ranges are set aside, review 3 R3-1). Any other ordinal before a year, and any ordinal in a clause with a rank word, is a rank or a position, so "ranks second since 2001" is checked as a rank (review 3 n4);
+- a sentence- or clause-initial ordering word ("First,", "Second,", "Thirdly,", up to "fifth") followed by a comma is no number;
+- the measure's own name is no count (review 3 R3-2): "team(s)", "author(s)", "coauthor(s)", or "collaborator(s)" followed by "on", "in", "of", or "for" and then "one" or "a single" and "paper(s)", "publication(s)", "work(s)", or "article(s)" (for example "98 authors on one paper", "286 coauthors on a single paper") becomes "... on a paper"; "once again" and "twice as" are no counts. Elsewhere the words still count ("collaborated on one paper" is 1);
 - tens-unit compounds ("twenty-one", "ninety nine") become 21 to 99;
 - cardinals "zero" to "nineteen" and the tens "twenty" to "ninety" become digits; "one" directly before "of" or "another" is not a count.
 
+**Ranks and list positions (review 3 R3-1, n4, n5; review 2 n2).** A number is a rank or position when it is an ordinal (digits with st, nd, rd, or th, or a converted ordinal word), or when the measure name nearest to it is a rank word and not also a list word (so "top 10" is a list size, "ranks 5" and "#5" are ranks). Such a number binds only (a) to an asserted `rank.<k>.storedRank` (which needs `rank.<k>.researcherId` in a RANKING claim, the `rank` rule) whose stored value equals it, or (b), for an ordinal, to the index k of an asserted `hop.<k>.*`, `entry.<k>.*`, `member.<k>.*`, or `neighbor.<k>.*` fact (a value that passed `asserted-values`; summary and uncertainty lines use the kept claims' positions): "the second hop" with hop 2's facts, "the second most common topic" with `entry.2.*`. It never binds to a listed value (`rank.<k>.value`), a year, or a free note number, so "X ranks 5th" with `rank.1.value` 5 fails (review 2 n2). An unbound ordinal in a clause without a rank word fails as "ordinal k is not the position k of an asserted hop, entry, member, or neighbor fact", never as an unbound rank; any other unbound rank fails as "k is a rank, and no asserted rank.<k>.storedRank of its researcher binds it". Ranking positions (`rank.<k>`) are not list positions: a rank in a ranking always needs its stored rank and researcher.
+
 "a" and "an" are articles, never 1: "a path", "a paper", and "coauthored a paper" are existential, true for any count of at least one, so counting them as 1 would remove correct claims. A count stated only by an article (for example "have a joint publication" against a stored 0) is therefore not caught by the number rule; a claim of that kind must still assert its values, which are checked. Other "no" phrases ("no stored value", "no path", "no language model") are not numbers.
 
 **Asserted names (ruling 29, N2).** The researcher names a claim may write are the researchers of the cited graph items whose facts its values assert with an equal value (window keys assert no researcher): for a `hop.<k>.*` key, hop k's from and to; for a `rank.<k>.*` key, rank k's researcher; for an edge, its two endpoints; for a path, its source and target, or every researcher on it for `researcherIds`; for a community, its returned members; for a facet, its subject and entries; for a metric, change, component, or identity item, its researcher. Ranking list facts (metric, sizes) and works name nobody. These researchers' resolved names and candidate display names are allowed too. A DESCRIPTIVE claim may also name its resolved subjects (it is context only). Every name in `researcherNames` (graph display names, resolved names, candidates, their top coauthors, other-name matches, and community members) written in the text, matched as whole words and longest first, must be allowed; text inside the cited works' titles is not checked (a name in a title is title text). So a name known only through resolution candidates or their top coauthors never verifies a claim that cites other evidence, and a claim with no values may name nobody. AMBIGUOUS and UNKNOWN_RESEARCHER answers never reach verification (section 13) and are unchanged.
 
-**Names (ruling 20).** After removing every bundle text (graph and semantic facts, resolved names, candidates, their coauthors and institutions, window names), any remaining run of two or more capitalized tokens (`RulePlanner.names`) that is not part of a bundle text is a name-like span and makes `identity` FAIL. Each span is looked up once per answer through the EntityLinkCache read port (`kv.entity-link`, limit 1, never a full load) only to say which it is: a corpus researcher who is not in the evidence, or not a researcher, title, venue, topic, or institution of the evidence.
+**Names (ruling 20).** After removing every bundle text of two or more words (graph and semantic facts, resolved names, candidates, their coauthors and institutions, window names), any remaining run of two or more capitalized tokens (`RulePlanner.names`) that is not itself a whole bundle text is a name-like span and makes `identity` FAIL. Review 2 n3 (verifier-v4): a one-word bundle text is no longer removed first, so it cannot cut a span such as "Alan Turing" down to one word, and a span that is only part of a longer bundle text (a name inside a title, "Wei Wang" inside "Wei Wang 0001") is no longer accepted. Each span is looked up once per answer through the EntityLinkCache read port (`kv.entity-link`, limit 1, never a full load) only to say which it is: a corpus researcher who is not in the evidence, or not a researcher, title, venue, topic, or institution of the evidence.
 
 **Status.** Any CONFLICT gives CONFLICTING. Otherwise any FAIL gives UNSUPPORTED. Otherwise any MISSING with at least one PASS gives PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED, and MISSING with no PASS gives UNSUPPORTED. All PASS gives VERIFIED. A DESCRIPTIVE claim always has a `context-only` MISSING or FAIL, so it is never VERIFIED, and every VERIFIED claim cites graph evidence (ruling 19). Actions: VERIFIED is KEPT. PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED is LABELLED with the uncertainty "Partially supported: <missing facts>", or "Context only: descriptive text retrieved by vector similarity; it is not verified against the graph." UNSUPPORTED and CONFLICTING are REMOVED from the answer and stay in the `VerificationReport`. The final claims are core `AnswerClaim`s (`answer-claim-v1`) with their status.
 
-**Summary and uncertainty lines (ruling 20).** They get the claims' numbers, windows, names, and titles rules. Their numbers must be bound to facts the kept claims asserted (an uncertainty line may also use the numbers of the fixed note lines), their windows must be the answer's window or a kept claim's window (an uncertainty line may also use year ranges of the fixed notes), every researcher name in them must be one the kept claims may write (asserted names above; ruling 29, for summary and uncertainty lines alike), and no name-like span may name anyone outside the bundle. A failing summary is replaced by the kept claims' texts in order. Failing model uncertainty lines are left out and replaced by the fixed line "<n> drafted uncertainty line(s) were left out because they failed verification." The fixed-template lines (window note, identity links, missing evidence) are always kept.
+**Summary and uncertainty lines (ruling 20).** They get the claims' numbers, windows, names, and titles rules. +**Removal records (review 3 n7).** `Verifier.verify` returns, besides the report, every removed claim (`RemovedClaim`: claimId, kind, text, status, and every check that did not pass) and every left-out model line (`DroppedLine`: part `SUMMARY` or `UNCERTAINTY`, text, and its reasons, for example "5 is not an asserted fact of a cited item" or "names X, whom no kept claim may name"). They go only into the audit object (section 14), never into a log line, the answer, or a service response.

+- Number words (rulings 29 and 31) cover the list in section 12 only. Not covered: "a"/"an" as a count (a deliberate choice, section 12), number words above 99 or with "hundred", fractions and percentages in words ("half", "a third"), ordinals beyond "ninety-ninth" (no stored rank or list is that long), and zero phrasings outside the list (for example "have yet to coauthor", "do not share any paper"). Only "first" before a year, in a clause with no rank word, is a time marker, so "ranks second in 2015" and "is second since 2001" are checked. "one" before "of" or "another" is skipped, so "one of the 50" checks only the 50. A list position is checked against the index k of the asserted list fact only: "the second hop" with hop 2's facts verifies whatever ordering the text implies, and a community member or facet entry written with a rank word ("ranks second among her topics") binds to its list position (the stored order) rather than failing.
+- Number words remove some correct model claims: "never collaborated" against a no-edge item binds only if the claim asserts `edgeExists` false or a joint count of 0; "no coauthors in common" becomes a 0 that no fact holds; "the first time" (no year after it), "the fifth paper" (no asserted list fact 5), or "one hop away" are counted; a measure phrase outside the section 12 list ("the most authors of any single paper") still counts its "single"-free number words. That errs toward removal. The measure-name phrase "on one paper" is recognised only after a team, author, coauthor, or collaborator word, so "Ada and Grace collaborated on a single paper" still states 1.
+- The added measure names (review 3 n6) make two families nearer: "joint publications" names both a pair's `jointPublicationCount` and a researcher's `weightedDegree`, and "coauthors per paper" names `averageCoauthorsPerPaper` as well as the coauthor families. +- Name-like spans (review 2 n3): a span written next to a one-word bundle text ("IPDPS Workshops", where only "IPDPS" is stored) or a shortened display name ("Paolo Romano" for "Paolo Romano 0002") is now flagged and the claim removed. That errs toward removal.
 - Asserted names (ruling 29): a model claim that names a researcher whose facts it cites but does not assert in `values` (for example a publication claim citing an edge and a work, with only the work ID in values, that names the pair) is removed. A community or facet claim may name any returned member or entry once it asserts one of the item's facts. A name inside a cited title is skipped. Names are matched as whole words against the bundle's researcher names, so a name spelled differently from every bundle name is caught only by the name-like span rule, and a single-word surname is not caught.
 
+- Corpus window summaries (work order 9): the node basis is DBLP-authored researchers only, communities did not converge in any stored window (10-round cap), and the windows differ in length; each is stated as an uncertainty line, but a model may still omit it from its claims. The stored community threshold is `communityMinSize` (3); a question with another threshold ("size 5 or more") gets the count at 3 with the threshold stated, and a model claim writing 5 is removed. A model that writes a singleton count as "researchers with no coauthors" has it read as "0 coauthors" (the zero rule of section 12), so the claim is removed; "singleton researchers" or "isolated researchers" verifies. Percentages ("69.4%") are not converted, so a share written as a percentage is removed. The sign of a delta is not checked against "larger" or "smaller" in the text (as for every stored change).
+- Live verifier removals reviewed in work order 9 (ruling 19b) and kept by design: a topic name taken from a cited profile text is a name-like span that is not a whole bundle text (review 2 n3), so a DESCRIPTIVE claim listing topics from a profile is removed; a RANKING claim that writes its position in a filtered list ("ranks second") where the stored rank differs is removed (stored ranks only, review 2 n2); a PUBLICATION claim that names the researcher whose maximum-team paper it is, but asserts only the work's facts, is removed (ruling 29 N2).
 - Planner-v2 (ruling 30) is tested only on recorded-shape fixtures. The step-1 audits keep the failed rules but not the model's window fields, so the rule-7 fixture (NAMED `2015-2026` with its years filled in) is a reconstruction. Any other rule-7 shape now records its exact fault (`Windows.requestProblems`). A failure reason longer than `LlmCaller.MaxReason` (600 characters with the shape) is cut in the audit, but not in the retry prompt. The live run is the real test.

+## 23. Service-facing API (Phase 10 work order 1)
+
+`runtime/RuntimeApi.scala` is the plain Scala API the Phase 10 services call (no HTTP; routes add no retrieval, verification, query building, or store logic, Phase 10 ruling 3):
+
+| Function | Use |
+|---|---|
+| `answer(request: QueryRequest, ports: RuntimePorts, setup: RuntimeSetup, deadlineMs: Long): Either[RuntimeFailure, RuntimeResult]` | one structured request (the POST /v1/query fields) under a deadline of `deadlineMs` from now; `Workflow.answer(..., Some(deadlineMs))` |
+| `audit(queryId: String, setup: RuntimeSetup, audits: ObjectReadPort): Either[String, Option[AuditRecord]]` | GET /v1/explain: the audit object of this runtime run (`Audit.auditKey(setup.snapshot, setup.runId, queryId)`) |
+| `storedAnswer(queryId: String, setup: RuntimeSetup, audits: ObjectReadPort): Either[String, Option[RuntimeAnswer]]` | the stored answer object (for a job result) |
+| `withoutModelText(a: AuditRecord): AuditRecord` | the audit with the text of removed claims and left-out lines blanked (Phase 10 ruling 8) |
+| `llmStubbed(a: AuditRecord): Boolean` | whether no language model was used (plan source `RULES`) |
+| `evidence(queryId: String, evidenceId: String, records: KeyValueReadPort, objects: ObjectReadPort): Either[String, Option[EvidenceItem]]` | GET /v1/evidence: the EvidenceBundle `EV#` item of a query |
+| `evidenceOf(item: Item, objects: ObjectReadPort): Either[String, EvidenceItem]` | one `EV#` item as an `EvidenceItem`: inline `payload`, or the `payloadRef` object whose sha256 must equal `payloadHash` |
+
+- `RuntimeResult` holds `answer`, `audit`, `bundle` (None when the answer abstained before stage 7), `written`, and `deadlinePassed`: the deadline had passed when the answer was finished, either because the workflow abstained at the deadline (`BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`) or because a model attempt ran past it. The audit is written in both cases. `RuntimeFailure.deadlinePassed` marks the rare failure before any plan exists.
+- The deadline is cooperative: it is checked before each stage, store read, embedding, vector query, and model attempt (section 2). A call already running is not interrupted, so one model attempt can overrun it by up to `runtime.llm.timeout-ms`; a hard timeout on the request, if wanted, is the service's.
+- Lookups return `Right(None)` for an ID that is not `qry-<32 hex>` or `ev-<32 hex>`, so no store key is built from other text, and `Left` for a stored object that does not decode or a payload whose hash differs. `audit` and `storedAnswer` find the queries of the setup's runtime run only (the run ID changes with the prompt, model, store, and verifier versions, section 14).
+- The EvidenceBundle table is keyed by query ID and `EV#<evidenceId>`. A lookup by evidence ID alone needs a storage read with an index from evidence ID to the item (storage-engineer, Phase 10 work order 4); `evidenceOf` then decodes the item it returns.
+- `records` is a read port over the runtime tables (locally `FileRuntimeRecords.reader`); `RuntimePorts.records` is the create-only writer.
+- `RuntimePorts.windowSummaries: Option[WindowSummarySource] = None` (Phase 10 work order 9) is the last field, with a default, so existing callers compile unchanged and keep the corpus abstention. To serve corpus answers, a composition builds `WindowSummarySource(k, port)` with `k = S3Layout.windowSummary(snapshot, loadRun)` and `port` from `WindowSummaries.open(<object reader>, k, cfg.graph.windows)` (a `Left` should stop startup), and adds `window-summary=<k>` to the `Workflow.runIdFor` inputs. The services do this (Phase 10 work order 11, `Composition.windowSummaries`) when `runtime.window-summary.load-run` is set (section 20); graph-evidence-api adds the run ID input without opening the object (repair-01 m3).

+    // Corpus window summaries (work order 9, F2): every value is the stored row's, with the row's
+    // provenance (its Spark run) and window; `scope` CORPUS tells them from a researcher's items.
+    def corpusFacts(s: WindowSummary): Map[String, String] =
+      win(s.window) ++ Map(
+        "scope" -> "CORPUS",
+        "researchers" -> s.researchers.toString,
+        "years" -> s.years.toString,
+        "nodeBasis" -> s.nodeBasis,
+        "metricVersion" -> s"${s.schemaVersion}@${s.provenance.runId}"
+      )
+    def corpusItem(t: EvidenceType, kind: String, s: WindowSummary, facts: Map[String, String]) =
+      item(
+        t,
+        Seq("CORPUS", s"$kind#${s.window}"),
+        s.provenance.runId,
+        (s.largestComponentId.toVector ++ s.largestCommunityId.toVector)
+          .filter(id => facts.values.exists(_ == id)),
+        s"${g.corpusSource}#${s.window}",
+        corpusFacts(s) ++ facts,
+        s.provenance
+      )
+    def componentFacts(s: WindowSummary) = Map(
+      "components" -> s.components.toString,
+      "largestComponentSize" -> s.largestComponentSize.toString,
+      "singletonResearchers" -> s.singletonResearchers.toString,
+      "coauthorEdges" -> s.coauthorEdges.toString
+    ) ++ s.largestComponentId.map("largestComponentId" -> _) ++
+      s.largestComponentFraction.map("largestComponentFraction" -> canon(_))
+    def communityFacts(s: WindowSummary) = Map(
+      "communities" -> s.communities.toString,
+      "largestCommunitySize" -> s.largestCommunitySize.toString,
+      "singletonCommunities" -> s.singletonCommunities.toString,
+      "communityMinSize" -> s.communityMinSize.toString,
+      "communitiesAtLeastMinSize" -> s.communitiesAtLeastMinSize.toString,
+      "communityConverged" -> s.communityConverged.toString,
+      "communityRounds" -> s.communityRounds.toString
+    ) ++ s.largestCommunityId.map("largestCommunityId" -> _) ++
+      s.largestCommunityFraction.map("largestCommunityFraction" -> canon(_))
+    val corpusComponents =
+      g.corpusComponents.map(s =>
+        corpusItem(EvidenceType.COMPONENT_RECORD, "COMPONENTS", s, componentFacts(s))
+      )
+    val corpusCommunities =
+      g.corpusCommunities.map(s =>
+        corpusItem(EvidenceType.COMMUNITY_RECORD, "COMMUNITIES", s, communityFacts(s))
+      )
+    // A stored pair of windows: from<Fact>, to<Fact>, and delta<Fact> = to - from (exact decimals of
+    // the two stored values), like a researcher's CHANGE# item.
+    val corpusChanges = g.corpusComparisons.map { (a, b) =>
+      val key = s"${a.window}>${b.window}"
+      def side(p: String, s: WindowSummary) =
+        (corpusFacts(s) ++ componentFacts(s) ++ communityFacts(s)).collect {
+          case (k, v) if !Set("scope", "nodeBasis", "metricVersion", "window").contains(k) =>
+            s"$p${k.head.toUpper}${k.tail}" -> v
+        } + (s"${p}Window" -> s.window)
+      val (from, to) = (side("from", a), side("to", b))
+      val deltas = from.toVector.flatMap { (k, v) =>
+        val f = k.stripPrefix("from")
+        for
+          x <- scala.util.Try(BigDecimal(v)).toOption
+          y <- to.get(s"to$f").flatMap(t => scala.util.Try(BigDecimal(t)).toOption)
+          if !f.endsWith("Year") && f != "CommunityMinSize"
+        yield s"delta$f" -> (y - x).bigDecimal.stripTrailingZeros.toPlainString
+      }
+      item(
+        EvidenceType.TEMPORAL_CHANGE,
+        Seq("CORPUS", s"CHANGE#$key"),
+        a.provenance.runId,
+        (a.largestComponentId.toVector ++ b.largestComponentId.toVector).distinct,
+        s"${g.corpusSource}#$key",
+        from ++ to ++ deltas ++ Map(
+          "window" -> key,
+          "scope" -> "CORPUS",
+          "nodeBasis" -> a.nodeBasis,
+          "metricVersion" -> s"${a.schemaVersion}@${a.provenance.runId}"
+        ),
+        a.provenance.copy(sourceRecordId = key)
+      )
+    }

+  private val SideKey = "(from|to)[A-Z]\\w*".r
+
+  /** The windows a cited item covers for a claim: its window, and for a stored comparison `a>b`
+    * also a when every fact the claim asserts from the item is a from<Fact>, or b when every one is
+    * a to<Fact> (verifier-v5, live B22: "in 2015-2026 ... had 251 distinct coauthors" asserting
+    * only the to side). A delta or any other fact of the pair keeps the comparison window only.
+    */
+  def windowsOf(d: ClaimDraft, e: EvidenceItem): Vector[String] =
+    e.facts.get("window").toVector.flatMap { w =>
+      val asserted = d.values.keys.filter(k => !isWindowKey(k) && e.facts.contains(k)).toVector
+      val sides = asserted.collect { case SideKey(p) => p }.distinct
+      val side = w.split('>') match
+        case Array(a, b) if sides.size == 1 && asserted.forall(k => SideKey.matches(k)) =>
+          Some(if sides.head == "from" then a else b)
+        case _ => None
+      w +: side.toVector
+    }

+  test(
+    "ruling 19a: a synthesis output cut off at max_tokens gets a retry that says so and asks for fewer claims"
+  ) {
+
+    /** A recorded client whose given invokes (1-based) stop with max_tokens, as the live B29 did.
+      */
+    final class CutOff(inner: RecordedLlm, cut: Set[Int]) extends LlmClient:
+      private var n = 0
+      def requestsSent: Long = inner.requestsSent
+      def invoke(r: LlmRequest): LlmResponse =
+        n += 1
+        val resp = inner.invoke(r)
+        if cut(n) then resp.copy(stopReason = Some(LlmCaller.MaxTokens)) else resp
+
+    // The shape of B29: output that stops inside the JSON (no model text is reused here).
+    val truncated = """{"schemaVersion": "synthesis-output-v3", "summary": "x", "claims": ["""
+    def run(cut: Set[Int], c: scholarchain.core.config.ScholarChainConfig = cfg) =
+      val inner = RecordedLlm(Vector(Right("{}"), Right("{}"), Right(truncated), Right(truncated)))
+      withWorld(Some(CutOff(inner, cut)), c) { w =>
+        val a = w.ask(q).fold(f => fail(s"$f"), identity)
+        (inner.requests, a)
+      }
+    val (requests, a) = run(Set(3, 4))
+    val retry = requests(3).user
+    val line =
+      "- output-cut-off: the output was cut off at the output token limit (stop reason max_tokens) " +
+        "before the JSON was complete; return at most 10 claims, one sentence each"
+    assert(retry.contains(line), retry)
+    assert(retry.indexOf(line) < retry.indexOf("- output-is-json"))
+    // No token limit or budget is raised: the retry has the same max_tokens.
+    assert(requests(3).maxTokens == cfg.runtime.synthesis.maxOutputTokens)
+    assert(requests(2).maxTokens == requests(3).maxTokens)
+    val synth = a.audit.llmCalls.filter(_.stage == Stage.ANSWER_SYNTHESIS)
+    assert(synth.map(_.outcome) == Vector(CallOutcome.INVALID_OUTPUT, CallOutcome.INVALID_OUTPUT))
+    assert(synth.head.failure.exists(_.contains("stop reason max_tokens")))
+    assert(a.answer.abstention.map(_.reason).contains(AbstentionReason.BUDGET_EXHAUSTED))
+    // Half of runtime.synthesis.max-claims, from configuration.
+    val (seven, _) = run(Set(3), configWith("runtime.synthesis.max-claims = 7"))
+    assert(seven(3).user.contains("return at most 3 claims, one sentence each"))
+    // Output that stopped at end_turn is not called cut off.
+    val (ended, _) = run(Set.empty)
+    assert(!ended(3).user.contains("output-cut-off") && ended(3).user.contains("- output-is-json"))
+  }

+  /** A FACET_RECORD of Grace Hopper's topics (three entries), added to a context's bundle. */
+  private def withFacet(c: Verifier.Context, extra: Map[String, String] = Map.empty) =
+    val base = c.bundle.graphEvidence.find(_.evidenceType == EvidenceType.METRIC_RECORD).get
+    val facet = base.copy(
+      evidenceId = ev(EvidenceType.FACET_RECORD, Seq(grace, "TOPIC", "all-years", "n=3"), graphRun),
+      evidenceType = EvidenceType.FACET_RECORD,
+      subjectIds = Vector(grace, "TOPIC", "all-years", "n=3"),
+      facts = Map(
+        "subjectId" -> grace,
+        "displayName" -> "Grace Hopper",
+        "window" -> "all-years",
+        "basis" -> "AUTHORED works",
+        "truncated" -> "false",
+        "entry.1.id" -> "openalex:T1",
+        "entry.1.name" -> "Cloud Computing Systems",
+        "entry.1.count" -> "41",
+        "entry.2.id" -> "openalex:T2",
+        "entry.2.name" -> "Fault Tolerance",
+        "entry.2.count" -> "12",
+        "entry.3.id" -> "openalex:T3",
+        "entry.3.name" -> "Compilers",
+        "entry.3.count" -> "5"
+      ) ++ extra
+    )
+    (c.copy(bundle = c.bundle.copy(graphEvidence = c.bundle.graphEvidence :+ facet)), facet)
+
+  private def detail(d: ClaimDraft, c: Verifier.Context, rule: String) =
+    Verifier.check(d, c).checks.filter(_.rule == rule).map(_.detail).mkString("; ")
+
+  test("R3-1: a list-position ordinal binds to the index k of an asserted hop or entry") {
+    context(pathQ) { (c, _) =>
+      val p = c.bundle.graphEvidence.find(_.evidenceType == EvidenceType.GRAPH_PATH).get
+      def hop(k: Int) = Verifier.HopFacts.map(f => s"hop.$k.$f" -> p.facts(s"hop.$k.$f")).toMap
+      // Review 3 R3-1 input (B10 shape): the second hop, all five hop 2 facts asserted.
+      val second = draft(
+        ClaimKind.PATH_HOP,
+        "The second hop links Grace Hopper and Edsger Dijkstra with 1 joint publication in 2012.",
+        Vector(p.evidenceId),
+        hop(2),
+        subjects = Vector.empty
+      )
+      assert(status(second, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(second, c))
+      // The wrong position fails as a position, never as an unbound rank.
+      val wrong = second.copy(text = second.text.replace("second", "third"))
+      assert(status(wrong, c) == UNSUPPORTED && failed(wrong, c) == Set("numbers-in-text"))
+      assert(detail(wrong, c, "numbers-in-text").contains("position k"))
+      assert(!detail(wrong, c, "numbers-in-text").contains("rank"))
+      val first = draft(
+        ClaimKind.PATH_HOP,
+        "The first hop links Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper with two joint publications, first in 2001 and last in 2002.",
+        Vector(p.evidenceId),
+        hop(1),
+        subjects = Vector.empty
+      )
+      assert(status(first, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(first, c))
+      // Both hops in one claim: either position may be written.
+      val both = first.copy(
+        text = "The first hop has 2 joint publications and the second hop has 1 joint publication.",
+        values = hop(1) ++ hop(2)
+      )
+      assert(status(both, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(both, c))
+    }
+    context(pairQ) { (c0, _) =>
+      val (c, facet) = withFacet(c0)
+      // Review 3 R3-1 input (B25 shape): the second most common topic, entry.2 asserted.
+      val topic = draft(
+        ClaimKind.FACET,
+        "Fault Tolerance is Grace Hopper's second most common topic with 12 papers in all-years.",
+        Vector(facet.evidenceId),
+        Map("entry.2.name" -> "Fault Tolerance", "entry.2.count" -> "12"),
+        subjects = Vector(grace)
+      )
+      assert(status(topic, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(topic, c))
+      assert(status(topic.copy(text = topic.text.replace("second", "third")), c) == UNSUPPORTED)
+      assert(
+        status(topic.copy(text = topic.text.replace("second most", "fiftieth most")), c) ==
+          UNSUPPORTED
+      )
+      // A year range after "first" (blanked before the conversion in verifier-v3) is a time marker.
+      val years = edgeClaim.copy(
+        text =
+          "Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper first collaborated in 2001-2002 and have 2 joint publications in all-years.",
+        values = edgeClaim.values ++ Map(
+          "firstCollaborationYear" -> "2001",
+          "lastCollaborationYear" -> "2002"
+        )
+      )
+      assert(status(years, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(years, c))
+    }
+  }
+
+  test("R3-1: an ordering word is no count, and an ordinal outside a ranking is no rank") {
+    import Verifier.wordsToDigits as w
+    assert(w("First, they met. Second, they wrote; Third, done.").forall(!_.isDigit))
+    assert(w("Firstly, x.\nSecondly, y.").forall(!_.isDigit))
+    // Mid-sentence ordinals are still positions or ranks.
+    assert(w("the first, second, and third hops") == "the 1st, 2nd, and 3rd hops")
+    context(pairQ) { (c, r) =>
+      val kept = edgeClaim
+      val v = lines(
+        c,
+        r,
+        kept,
+        "First, Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper have 2 joint publications.",
+        Vector.empty
+      )
+      assert(v.summary == "First, Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper have 2 joint publications.")
+    }
+  }
+
+  test("R3-2: the measure name 'on one paper' and fixed phrases are not counts") {
+    import Verifier.wordsToDigits as w
+    assert(w("98 authors on one paper") == "98 authors on a paper")
+    assert(w("286 coauthors on a single paper") == "286 coauthors on a paper")
+    assert(w("the largest team on one paper") == "the largest team on a paper")
+    assert(w("once again; twice as many coauthors").forall(!_.isDigit))
+    // Outside the measure name, the same words still count.
+    assert(w("they collaborated on one paper") == "they collaborated on 1 paper")
+    assert(w("they wrote a single paper together") == "they wrote 1 paper together")
+    context(pairQ) { (c, _) =>
+      // Review 3 R3-2 input (B05 shape): maximumTeamSize with "authors on one paper".
+      val team = draft(
+        ClaimKind.METRIC_VALUE,
+        "Ada Lovelace's largest team had 2 authors on one paper in all-years.",
+        Vector(adaMetric),
+        Map("maximumTeamSize" -> "2")
+      )
+      assert(status(team, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(team, c))
+      assert(
+        status(team.copy(text = team.text.replace("one paper", "a single paper")), c) == VERIFIED
+      )
+      assert(
+        status(team.copy(text = team.text.replace("2 authors", "3 authors")), c) == UNSUPPORTED
+      )
+    }
+    context("Which researchers have the most distinct coauthors?") { (c, _) =>
+      // Review 3 R3-2 input (B04 shape): a correct ranking claim with "coauthors on one paper".
+      val ranking = c.bundle.graphEvidence.find(_.evidenceType == EvidenceType.RANKING_RECORD).get
+      assert(ranking.facts("rank.1.researcherId") == grace && ranking.facts("rank.1.value") == "2")
+      val b04 = draft(
+        ClaimKind.RANKING,
+        "Grace Hopper ranks first with 2 distinct coauthors on one paper in all-years.",
+        Vector(ranking.evidenceId),
+        Map("rank.1.researcherId" -> grace, "rank.1.storedRank" -> "1", "rank.1.value" -> "2"),
+        subjects = Vector(grace)
+      )
+      assert(status(b04, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(b04, c))
+      val again = b04.copy(text = "Once again, Grace Hopper ranks first with 2 distinct coauthors.")
+      assert(status(again, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(again, c))
+    }
+  }
+
+  test(
+    "n4 and review 2 n2: a rank is checked against a stored rank, never a year or a listed value"
+  ) {
+    context(pairQ) { (c, r) =>
+      val kept = edgeClaim.copy(
+        text =
+          "Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper have 2 joint publications in all-years, first in 2001.",
+        values = edgeClaim.values ++ Map("firstCollaborationYear" -> "2001")
+      )
+      assert(status(kept, c) == VERIFIED)
+      def say(s: String) = lines(c, r, kept, s, Vector.empty).summary
+      // Review 3 n4 input: a single year after the ordinal no longer hides the rank.
+      assert(say("Grace Hopper ranks second since 2001.") == kept.text)
+      assert(say("Grace Hopper is second since 2001.") == kept.text)
+      // A measure word nearer the year binds the year as a year; the ordinal must still be a rank.
+      assert(say("Grace Hopper ranks second for joint publications since 2001.") == kept.text)
+      assert(say("Grace Hopper is second for joint publications since 2001.") == kept.text)
+      assert(say("Grace Hopper ranks first for joint publications since 2001.") == kept.text)
+      // "first" before a year without a rank word stays a time marker.
+      val time = "Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper first coauthored in 2001."
+      assert(say(time) == time)
+    }
+    context("Which researchers have the most distinct coauthors?") { (c, _) =>
+      val ranking = c.bundle.graphEvidence.find(_.evidenceType == EvidenceType.RANKING_RECORD).get
+      // Review 2 n2 input: rank 1's value is 2, so "2nd" equals a listed value but is not a rank.
+      val n2 = draft(
+        ClaimKind.RANKING,
+        "Grace Hopper ranks 2nd by distinct coauthors in all-years.",
+        Vector(ranking.evidenceId),
+        Map("rank.1.value" -> "2", "rank.1.researcherId" -> grace),
+        subjects = Vector(grace)
+      )
+      assert(status(n2, c) == UNSUPPORTED && failed(n2, c) == Set("numbers-in-text"))
+      assert(
+        status(
+          n2.copy(text = "Grace Hopper ranks 2 by distinct coauthors in all-years."),
+          c
+        ) == UNSUPPORTED
+      )
+      assert(
+        status(
+          n2.copy(text = "Grace Hopper is #2 by distinct coauthors in all-years."),
+          c
+        ) == UNSUPPORTED
+      )
+      // The value written as a value still verifies.
+      val value = n2.copy(text = "Grace Hopper has 2 distinct coauthors in all-years.")
+      assert(status(value, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(value, c))
+    }
+  }
+
+  test("n5: ordinals up to fiftieth (and beyond) are checked") {
+    import Verifier.wordsToDigits as w
+    assert(
+      w(
+        "eleventh, twelfth, nineteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, forty-ninth, fiftieth, sixtieth"
+      ) ==
+        "11th, 12th, 19th, 20th, 21st, 49th, 50th, 60th"

+      val k = (1 to 3).find(i => ranking.facts(s"rank.$i.researcherId") == grace).get
+      val d = draft(
+        ClaimKind.RANKING,

+        Vector(ranking.evidenceId),

+        subjects = Vector(grace)
+      )
+      assert(status(d, c) == UNSUPPORTED && failed(d, c) == Set("numbers-in-text"))
+    }
+  }

+  test("review 2 n3: a name inside a longer bundle text, or cut by a one-word text, is flagged") {
+    context(pairQ) { (c0, _) =>
+      val (c, _) = withFacet(
+        c0,
+        Map("entry.4.name" -> "Barbara Liskov Systems Group", "entry.5.name" -> "Turing")
+      )
+      // Barbara Liskov is a corpus researcher outside this bundle; only a longer text holds her name.
+      assert(!c.researcherNames("Barbara Liskov"))
+      assert(
+        Verifier.unknownNames("Ada Lovelace met Barbara Liskov.", c).map(_._1) == Vector(
+          "Barbara Liskov"
+        )
+      )
+      // "Turing" alone is a bundle text; it no longer cuts "Alan Turing" to one word.
+      assert(
+        Verifier.unknownNames("Ada Lovelace met Alan Turing.", c).map(_._1) == Vector("Alan Turing")
+      )
+      // Whole bundle texts still pass.
+      assert(Verifier.unknownNames("Barbara Liskov Systems Group studies Turing.", c).isEmpty)
+      assert(Verifier.unknownNames("Fault Tolerance and Cloud Computing Systems.", c).isEmpty)
+    }
+  }

+  test("n7: the audit records removed claims and left-out lines with their reasons") {
+    val bad = edgeClaim.copy(
+      claimId = "claim-1",
+      text = "Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper have three joint publications in all-years."
+    )
+    val good = edgeClaim.copy(claimId = "claim-2")
+    val synth = SynthesisOutput(
+      RuntimeSchemas.SynthesisOutput,
+      "They have 5 joint publications.",
+      Vector(bad, good),
+      Vector("Alan Turing may be a coauthor.", "The window is all-years.")
+    )
+    val llm = RecordedLlm(Vector(Right("{}"), Right("{}"), Right(write(synth))))
+    withWorld(Some(llm)) { w =>
+      val r = ok(w.ask(pairQ))
+      val a = r.audit
+      assert(a.schemaVersion == "rag-audit-v3")
+      assert(!RuntimeApi.llmStubbed(a)) // a model client was given
+      assert(
+        a.removedClaims.map(x => (x.claimId, x.text, x.status)) == Vector(
+          ("claim-1", bad.text, UNSUPPORTED)
+        )
+      )
+      assert(a.removedClaims.head.failedChecks.map(_.rule) == Vector("numbers-in-text"))
+      assert(
+        a.droppedLines.map(x => (x.part, x.text)) ==
+          Vector(
+            LinePart.SUMMARY -> synth.summary,
+            LinePart.UNCERTAINTY -> "Alan Turing may be a coauthor."
+          )
+      )
+      assert(a.droppedLines(0).reasons.exists(_.contains("5 is not an asserted fact")))
+      assert(a.droppedLines(1).reasons.exists(_.contains("Alan Turing")))
+      // The written audit object holds them; the answer does not; the service view drops the text.
+      val stored = RuntimeApi.audit(a.queryId, w.setup, w.audit.reader).fold(fail(_), identity).get
+      assert(stored == a)
+      assert(!write(r.answer).contains("three joint publications"))
+      val view = RuntimeApi.withoutModelText(a)
+      assert(view.removedClaims.forall(_.text.isEmpty) && view.droppedLines.forall(_.text.isEmpty))
+      assert(view.removedClaims.head.failedChecks == a.removedClaims.head.failedChecks)
+    }
+  }

+  test("work order 9, live B18 shape: a number written with digit-group commas is one number") {
+    assert(Verifier.ungroup("12,345 and 1,234,567.") == "12345 and 1234567.")
+    // Only exact groups of three digits: a year list or a short group stays apart.
+    assert(Verifier.ungroup("2001,2002 and 1,23 and 12,3456") == "2001,2002 and 1,23 and 12,3456")
+    context(pairQ) { (c0, _) =>
+      // Ada's metric item with a component size of 12345 (the failure shape: a COMPONENT claim
+      // citing a metric item, its componentSize written with a comma).
+      val b = c0.bundle
+      val c = c0.copy(bundle =
+        b.copy(graphEvidence =
+          b.graphEvidence.map(e =>
+            if e.evidenceId == adaMetric then e.copy(facts = e.facts + ("componentSize" -> "12345"))
+            else e
+          )
+        )
+      )
+      val size = Map("componentSize" -> "12345")
+      val claim = draft(
+        ClaimKind.COMPONENT,
+        "In all-years, Ada Lovelace is in a connected component of 12,345 researchers.",
+        Vector(adaMetric),
+        size
+      )
+      assert(status(claim, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(claim, c).checks)
+      // A wrong grouped number, or a malformed group, still fails.
+      val wrong = claim.copy(text =
+        "In all-years, Ada Lovelace is in a connected component of 12,346 researchers."
+      )
+      assert(status(wrong, c) == UNSUPPORTED)
+      assert(
+        Verifier
+          .check(wrong, c)
+          .checks
+          .exists(x => x.rule == "numbers-in-text" && x.detail.contains("12346 is not"))
+      )
+      val broken = claim.copy(text =
+        "In all-years, Ada Lovelace is in a connected component of 1,2345 researchers."
+      )
+      assert(status(broken, c) == UNSUPPORTED)
+    }
+  }

+  test(
+    "work order 9, live B22 shape: a claim on one side of a stored comparison may take that side's window"
+  ) {
+    context(pairQ) { (c0, r) =>
+      val changeEv =
+        ev(EvidenceType.TEMPORAL_CHANGE, Seq(grace, "CHANGE#2000-2014>2015-2026"), metricRun)
+      val change = scholarchain.core.EvidenceItem(
+        scholarchain.core.SchemaVersions.EvidenceItem,
+        changeEv,
+        EvidenceType.TEMPORAL_CHANGE,
+        Vector(grace),
+        None,
+        s"dynamodb:ResearcherMetricCache/$grace/CHANGE#2000-2014>2015-2026",
+        None,
+        None,
+        None,
+        None,
+        None,
+        Map(
+          "window" -> "2000-2014>2015-2026",
+          "fromWindow" -> "2000-2014",
+          "toWindow" -> "2015-2026",
+          "researcherId" -> grace,
+          "displayName" -> "Grace Hopper",
+          "fromDistinctCoauthorCount" -> "2",
+          "toDistinctCoauthorCount" -> "3",
+          "deltaDistinctCoauthorCount" -> "1"
+        ),
+        r.answer.response.provenance
+      )
+      val c = c0.copy(bundle = c0.bundle.copy(graphEvidence = c0.bundle.graphEvidence :+ change))
+      def d(text: String, window: String, values: Map[String, String]) =
+        draft(ClaimKind.METRIC_VALUE, text, Vector(changeEv), values, Some(window), Vector(grace))
+      val to = Map("toDistinctCoauthorCount" -> "3")
+      val from = Map("fromDistinctCoauthorCount" -> "2")
+      // The to side in the to window, and the from side in the from window, verify.
+      val toClaim = d("In 2015-2026, Grace Hopper had 3 distinct coauthors.", "2015-2026", to)
+      assert(status(toClaim, c) == VERIFIED, Verifier.check(toClaim, c).checks)
+      assert(
+        status(d("In 2000-2014, Grace Hopper had 2 distinct coauthors.", "2000-2014", from), c) ==
+          VERIFIED
+      )
+      // The other side's window, or a delta (a fact of the pair), keeps the comparison window only.
+      assert(
+        status(d("In 2000-2014, Grace Hopper had 3 distinct coauthors.", "2000-2014", to), c) ==
+          CONFLICTING
+      )
+      assert(
+        status(
+          d(
+            "In 2015-2026, Grace Hopper had 3 distinct coauthors, 1 more.",
+            "2015-2026",
+            to + ("deltaDistinctCoauthorCount" -> "1")
+          ),
+          c
+        ) == CONFLICTING
+      )
+      assert(
+        status(
+          d("In 2015-2026, Grace Hopper had 3 distinct coauthors.", "2015-2026", to ++ from),
+          c
+        ) == CONFLICTING
+      )
+      // The comparison window still verifies a claim on both sides.
+      assert(
+        status(
+          d(
+            "Grace Hopper's distinct coauthors went from 2 in 2000-2014 to 3 in 2015-2026.",
+            "2000-2014>2015-2026",
+            to ++ from
+          ),
+          c
+        ) == VERIFIED
+      )
+    }
+  }

+## CorpusWindowSummaryJob (Phase 10 work order 7: corpus-level window summaries, FU2)
+
+```text
+sbt "sparkAnalytics/runMain scholarchain.spark.CorpusWindowSummaryJob --input <temporal-summary-export run dir> \
+  --code-version <v> [--out <root>] [--checkpoint-dir <dir>] [--summary-min-community-size 3] [--attempt n]"
+```
+
+A separate, read-only stage over the Phase 6 chain: one row per configured window with the graph-level values the corpus questions need (B13, B15, B17, B24). It is not a conversion run and writes nothing into any input run. Values are recomputed with Spark from the base Phase 6 tables (group-bys on `window`; nothing but the 3 summary rows reaches the driver) and checked against the Phase 6 `temporal/window-summary`.
+
+- Input: one complete TemporalSummaryExportJob run. Its manifest names the community-path, researcher-analytics, and canonical-graph runs; the four run IDs must form one chain, and the configured `graph.windows` must equal the input's, or the run fails. Tables read (columns only): `analytics/researcher-metrics` (window, researcherId), `analytics/components` (window, componentId, componentSize), `analytics/communities` (window, communityId, communitySize), `graph/coauthor-edges` (window, jointPublicationCount), the export's `temporal/window-summary` (for the check only), and the community-path manifest's `counts.communityConverged` and `counts.communityIterations`.
+- Run ID: `StableIds.runId("spark-corpus-window-summary", code version, config version, [the four input runs and their sizes, graph policy, windows, summary-min-community-size], attempt)`.
+- Output (JobRun layout): `<out>/snapshot=<snapshot>/run=<runId>/corpus/window-summary` and `corpus/window-summary-violations`, one Parquet file each, plus `_manifest.json` (`counts.windowSummary` holds every row without provenance; `counts.integrityViolations` every violation) and `_stages.json`.
+- Phase 10 run: `scratch/runtime/wo7/spark/snapshot=2026-10-03/run=run-1de2293597c338227290eb382de16957/` (code version `src-db536eecfe78`, attempt 1), from export run `run-0375535e0174b7330b18a78988c07211`; 3 rows, 0 violations. The output root is under `scratch/runtime/` (Phase 10 ruling 14), not `scratch/spark/`.
+
+### Contract: `corpus/window-summary` (schema version `spark-corpus-window-summary-v1`)
+
+Grain: one row per configured window (3 rows: `all-years`, `2000-2014`, `2015-2026`). Key: `window`. Intended targets: dynamodb, s3. A benchmark window `all` is the first configured window, `all-years` (as BenchmarkPathJob). Every value is for the stated window only (inclusive years).
+
+| Column | Type | Definition |
+|---|---|---|
+| `window`, `windowStartYear`, `windowEndYear` | STRING, INT, INT | the window (inclusive years) |
+| `years` | INT | windowEndYear - windowStartYear + 1 |
+| `researchers` | BIGINT | graph nodes: researchers with a DBLP publication in the window (researcher-metrics rows); see `nodeBasis` |
+| `coauthorEdges` | BIGINT | coauthor edges (distinct researcher pairs) in the window |
+| `sumJointPublicationCount` | BIGINT | sum of `jointPublicationCount` over those edges |
+| `components` | BIGINT | connected components, singletons included (job 4) |
+| `largestComponentId`, `largestComponentSize` | STRING, BIGINT | the largest component (ties: smallest componentId); the ID resolves to `analytics/components` |
+| `largestComponentFraction` | DOUBLE | largestComponentSize / researchers (null when researchers = 0) |
+| `singletonResearchers` | BIGINT | researchers in a component of size 1, that is graph nodes with no coauthor edge in the window |
+| `communities` | BIGINT | label-propagation communities, size-1 communities included (job 6) |
+| `largestCommunityId`, `largestCommunitySize` | STRING, BIGINT | the largest community (ties: smallest communityId); the ID resolves to `analytics/communities` |
+| `largestCommunityFraction` | DOUBLE | largestCommunitySize / researchers (null when researchers = 0) |
+| `singletonCommunities` | BIGINT | communities of one member |
+| `communityMinSize`, `communitiesAtLeastMinSize` | INT, BIGINT | the threshold (`--summary-min-community-size`, default 3) and the number of communities with at least that many members |
+| `communityConverged`, `communityRounds` | BOOLEAN, INT | whether label propagation converged in this window, and the rounds run (the cap, 10, when not converged) |
+| `nodeBasis` | STRING | the fixed text saying who the nodes are (DBLP authorship only) |
+| `sourceRuns` | STRUCT | `temporalSummaryExportRunId`, `communityPathRunId`, `researcherAnalyticsRunId`, `canonicalGraphRunId`, `inputHadoopRunId`, `matchPolicyVersion` |
+| `provenance` | STRUCT | core Provenance names: `sourceDataset` DERIVED, `sourceRecordId` = window, `snapshot`, `runId` = this run, `policyVersion` = graph policy, `modelVersion` null |
+| `schemaVersion` | STRING | `spark-corpus-window-summary-v1` |
+
+Count columns are 0 (never null) for a window with no rows. A column change bumps the schema version.
+
+Benchmark use (fields that answer each question):
+
+| Question | Window | Fields |
+|---|---|---|
+| B13 components and largest share | 2000-2014 | `components`, `largestComponentFraction` (with `largestComponentSize`, `researchers`, `largestComponentId`) |
+| B15 researchers with no coauthors | all (`all-years`) | `singletonResearchers`, scoped by `nodeBasis` and `researchers` |
+| B17 communities of size 3 or more | 2000-2014 | `communitiesAtLeastMinSize` where `communityMinSize` = 3; `communityConverged` |
+| B24 largest component share, later vs earlier | 2015-2026 and 2000-2014 | `largestComponentFraction` of both rows (with `largestComponentSize`, `researchers`) |
+
+`corpus/window-summary-violations` (`spark-corpus-window-summary-violations-v1`; check, subjectId = window, relatedId = column, detail = `ours != other`):
+- `COMPONENT_MEMBERS_MISMATCH`, `COMMUNITY_MEMBERS_MISMATCH`: component (community) sizes of a window do not sum to `researchers`.
+- `PHASE6_WINDOW_SUMMARY_MISMATCH`: `researchers`, `coauthorEdges`, `sumJointPublicationCount`, `components`, `largestComponentSize`, `singletonResearchers`, `communities`, or `largestCommunitySize` (and `communitiesAtLeastMinSize` when both runs used the same threshold) differs from the Phase 6 `temporal/window-summary`, or the window has no Phase 6 row.
+
+Limitations:
+- Nodes are DBLP-authored researchers only, so `singletonResearchers` counts researchers with no DBLP coauthor in the window. Researchers known only from OpenAlex or Crossref are not nodes and are not counted at all.
+- Communities inherit job 6: label propagation stopped at the 10-round cap without converging in every window of the Phase 6 run (`communityConverged` false), so community counts and sizes depend on the cap and the tie-break.
+- Cross-window comparisons inherit job 9's inclusion limitation (`temporal/window-comparison.inclusionLimitation`): bounded DBLP subset, windows of 15 and 12 years, the last year possibly incomplete. `all-years` contains both other windows.
+
+Shuffle boundaries: one group-by on `window` per input table (metrics, edges, components, communities), one `row_number` window partitioned by `window` for each largest ID (at most one task per window; the all-years window holds 16,091 component and 38,034 community rows, so skew is bounded), the left joins of the five per-window results to the windows table, and the join to the Phase 6 summary for the check. No iteration, so no checkpoints (the checkpoint directory is set but stays empty).
+

+**Node lookup (Phase 10).** `GET /v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}` uses `vertex(id)`, template `graph.vertex` (`MATCH (n) WHERE id(n) = $id RETURN n`): one fixed text, the ID bound as `$id`, at most one row, behind the query timeout hint. It returns any label (Researcher, Work, Venue, Institution, Topic, Community) with its provenance properties. No new template was needed. The service checks `IdFormat.isNodeId` first.
+
+**Neighbor pages (Phase 10 ruling 8).** The walk order is fixed for an unchanged store: level by level; within a level, discovery order, meaning the previous level in its own order, read in batches of `graph-read.id-batch-size`, each batch's `graph.expand` rows by edge ID, then node ID. A walk capped at n nodes keeps exactly the first n nodes of any walk with a larger cap. So page (offset, limit) is the walk capped at offset + limit with the first offset nodes dropped, and consecutive pages concatenate to the whole walk (tested). The total cap `offset + limit <= graph-read.max-results` (1000) bounds every page; the store reads at most offset + limit nodes. `truncated` on a page means more nodes may follow, so the service may offer the next cursor (offset + limit) while that stays within the cap. The cursor itself (opaque encoding, binding it to the node, direction, depth, and edge types) belongs to the service.
 
 **Shortest path.** Neptune openCypher does not support `shortestPath()` ([openCypher compliance](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/feature-opencypher-compliance.html), accessed 2026-11-06), so the port runs a bounded bidirectional breadth-first search over `graph.frontier`, in ID batches. Full levels are expanded from the side with the smaller frontier until the two balls meet; that gives the length L. The path is then read back from the target with the Spark job 5 rule: each step takes the smallest neighbor whose distance from the source is one less (`modules/spark/JOBS.md`). Inside the source ball that distance is known. Beyond it, the shortest-path layers are built forward from the meeting layer over the neighbor lists read on the target side (a neighbor of a shortest-path node with the right target distance is itself on a shortest path). So a FOUND path equals the Spark path for the same pair and window. A single-source search was tried first: with the default `path-max-frontier` of 50,000 it stopped at SEARCH_LIMIT on all 54 benchmark pairs, even a 3-hop one, because hubs reach most of the largest component within a few hops. Statuses:
 - `UNKNOWN_RESEARCHER`: either end is not a Researcher vertex;
@@ -181,7 +186,7 @@ Rules for every template:
