+## Service state, evidence by ID, and the job queue (Phase 10 work order 4)
+
+Code: `ServiceState.scala`, `JobQueue.scala`, `Dynamo.scala` (`EvidenceLookupPort`), `live/ServiceLive.scala`, `live/LiveAws.scala` (`sqs`, `overridesWithTimeout`). Tests: `ServiceStateSpec`, `JobQueueSpec`, `GraphReadSpec` (neighbor pages), `LiveRequestSpec` (SQS client credentials). All tests are network-free; the live adapters run against stub SDK clients.
+
+### Service state (rulings 7 and 11)
+
+**Decision: one new table, `ServiceState`.** The existing runtime tables do not fit:
+- QuerySession and EvidenceBundle are create-only rag runtime records (the audit trail), and a job needs conditional state updates.
+- Their keys are (sessionId, turnKey) and (queryId, itemKey). `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}` has only the job ID, a one-way hash of the query ID, and an idempotency lookup has only the client's key.
+- Their item schema versions belong to rag-engineer.
+
+Phase 12 (infra-engineer) must create the table: on-demand, partition key `recordKey` (S), sort key `recordType` (S), TTL enabled on `expiresAt`. Physical name: `dynamodb.table-prefix` + `ServiceState`. The query-api and worker roles need `dynamodb:GetItem` and `dynamodb:PutItem` on it.
+
+| `recordType` | `recordKey` | Attributes | `schemaVersion` | Writer |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| `JOB` | the job ID, `StableIds.jobId(queryId)` | `queryId`, `state` (core `JobState`), `request` (canonical JSON of the POST body, a JSON object), `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, optional `stage` (`[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,63}`), `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `errorCode` (core `ErrorCode`) with `errorMessage` (1 to 500 characters), `resultRef` (S3 key), `expiresAt` | `service-job-v1` | `JobStore` |
+| `IDEMPOTENCY` | `idem-` + 64 hex of SHA-256 of the Idempotency-Key header value (the value itself is never stored) | `requestHash` (`sha256:<64 hex>` of the canonical body, computed by the service), `queryId`, `createdAt`, `expiresAt` | `service-idempotency-v1` | `IdempotencyStore` |
+
+Timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC strings with milliseconds. `expiresAt` is epoch seconds: createdAt + `services.ttl.job-hours` (168) for jobs, createdAt + `services.ttl.idempotency-hours` (24) for keys, fixed at creation. Reads hide expired items (DynamoDB deletes them only within a few days), and an expired key or job can be created again. Item budget: 96 KB, because a job holds its request and `services.max-body-bytes` is 64 KiB. Every other table keeps 32 KB. A write costs up to 96 WRU for the largest request; a typical request is under 1 KB.
+
+Job record rules (`ServiceStateContract.job`, checked on every write and every read):
+- `recordKey` equals `jobId(queryId)`;
+- `error` is present if and only if the state is FAILED;
+- `finishedAt` is present if and only if the state is final;
+- RUNNING and SUCCEEDED have `startedAt`, and QUEUED does not;
+- `resultRef` is allowed only on SUCCEEDED and must be a valid S3 key.
+
+The loader and the rag runtime writer refuse the table. `DynamoItemFile.tableDirs` refuses it as a load input.
+
+**Conditional writes.** Ports: `ServiceStateTable` (fake `LocalServiceStateTable`, live `DynamoServiceStateTable`), with the typed stores `JobStore` and `IdempotencyStore` over it.
+- **Create** (`JobStore.create`, `IdempotencyStore.claim`): PutItem with `attribute_not_exists(#pk) OR #e <= :now` and `ReturnValuesOnConditionCheckFailure = ALL_OLD`. A failed condition returns the stored record. The outcome is then:
+  - `Unchanged` for a job with the same queryId and request, or a key with the same requestHash (replay the stored queryId);
+  - `Conflict` otherwise. For a key this means 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT. Nothing is written.
+- **Transition** (`JobStore.transition(jobId, from, to, stage, error, resultRef)`):
+  - `Left` when `from.canMoveTo(to)` is false (only QUEUED->RUNNING, QUEUED->FAILED, RUNNING->SUCCEEDED, RUNNING->FAILED) or when the new record breaks a rule.
+  - Otherwise the store reads the job, then writes the whole new record with PutItem `#s = :from` (ALL_OLD on failure).
+  - `Moved(job)` on success; `Refused(current)` when the job is missing or not in `from`.
+  - The transition graph has no cycle, so a job in state `from` has not been written since it entered `from`, and the read-then-put cannot lose an update.
+  - A redelivered message's second QUEUED->RUNNING is `Refused`. So a job never runs twice (tested with 8 racing threads: exactly one `Moved`).
+  - RUNNING sets `startedAt`; SUCCEEDED and FAILED set `finishedAt`; `stage`, when given, replaces the stored one.
+- **Stuck RUNNING.** A worker that dies while RUNNING leaves the job RUNNING. The redelivered message's QUEUED->RUNNING is refused. The worker may then move it RUNNING->FAILED (for example once `receiveCount` reaches `services.queue.max-receive-count`) and delete the message. That policy belongs to the service.
+- **Retries (ruling 9).** Reads (GetItem) are retried by the `RetryPolicy` the caller passes (build it from `services.resilience`; the live adapters have no default policy). Conditional puts are never retried. After a `TransientStoreException` from a put, the outcome is unknown: read the record back.
+- **Result.** The rag workflow already writes the answer object (`RuntimeApi.storedAnswer(queryId)`). `resultRef` lets the worker also record that object's key on SUCCEEDED.
+
+**Fakes** (thread-safe; inject the clock):
+```scala
+LocalServiceStateTable(log: Option[java.nio.file.Path], now: () => java.time.Instant)
+JobStore(table: ServiceStateTable, ttlHours: Int, now: () => Instant)          // cfg.services.ttl.jobHours
+IdempotencyStore(table: ServiceStateTable, ttlHours: Int, now: () => Instant)  // cfg.services.ttl.idempotencyHours
+```
+- `log = None` is in memory only.
+- `log = Some(file)`, for example `scratch/services/<runId>/ServiceState.jsonl`, appends every accepted write as one item-file line before applying it, and replays the log on open; the last line of a key wins.
+- A torn last line (crash) is cut on open. Any other unreadable line stops the open with `IllegalStateException`.
+- One table instance can back both stores, and all services in one JVM should share it.
+
+### Evidence by ID (work order 1, item 5)
+
+**Decision: a GSI on EvidenceBundle over existing attributes.** No rag write-path change is needed, so nothing is blocked on rag-engineer.
+- Index `evidenceById` (`DynamoContract.EvidenceByIdIndex`): partition key `itemKey` (S), sort key `queryId` (S), projection ALL.
+- Phase 12 Terraform must add it to the EvidenceBundle table, and the graph-evidence-api role needs `dynamodb:Query` on `<table ARN>/index/evidenceById`.
+- An evidence ID is `H(evidenceType, runId, subjectIds)`, so the same fact in several queries of one run has one ID. `EvidenceLookupPort.evidenceItem(evidenceId)` returns the unexpired `EV#<evidenceId>` item of the smallest queryId. Decode it with rag `RuntimeApi.evidenceOf(item, objects)`.
+- A malformed evidence ID returns None and no key is built.
+- Live: Query `#k = :k` with filter `#e > :now`, ascending, one page of at most 100 evaluated items (shortcut). A GSI read is eventually consistent, so evidence written a moment ago may briefly be missing (404).
+- The `BUNDLE` header items all share the GSI partition `BUNDLE`, which takes one write per query. That is far below the per-partition write limit at the ruling 10 rates.
+- Fakes: `InMemoryDynamo.evidenceLookup` and `FileRuntimeRecords.evidenceLookup` scan the EvidenceBundle items in (queryId, itemKey) order.
+
+Audit by query ID is a rag read over `ObjectReadPort` (`RuntimeApi.audit`). Storage adds nothing for it.
+
+### Job queue (ruling 7)
+
+```scala
+final case class JobMessage(jobId: String, queryId: String)   // JobMessage.of(queryId); wire form job-message-v1
+final case class ReceivedJob(messageId: String, receiptHandle: String, receiveCount: Int,
+                             message: Either[String, JobMessage])
+trait JobQueuePort:
+  def send(message: JobMessage): String
+  def receive(maxMessages: Int, waitSeconds: Int, visibilitySeconds: Int): Vector[ReceivedJob]
+  def delete(receiptHandle: String): Boolean
+  def changeVisibility(receiptHandle: String, seconds: Int): Boolean
+InMemoryJobQueue(maxReceiveCount: Int, now: () => Instant = () => Instant.now())  // + sendRaw, deadLetters, size
+SqsJobQueue(client: SqsClient, queueUrl: String, timeoutMs: Long)                 // LiveAws.sqs(target, o)
+```
+- **Message body.** Exactly `{"schemaVersion":"job-message-v1","jobId","queryId"}`, with `jobId = StableIds.jobId(queryId)`. Any other body is received as `message = Left(...)`, a poison message: never run it.
+- **Bounds** (`JobQueueLimits`, the same in the fake and live): maxMessages 1 to 10, waitSeconds 0 to 20, visibility 0 to 43200 s. Pass `services.worker.visibility-timeout-seconds` on every receive, so the receive does not depend on the queue default.
+- **Fake semantics:**
+  - A receive hides each returned message until now + visibility, increments its receive count, and issues a new receipt handle.
+  - Only the latest handle deletes it or changes its visibility. A stale handle returns false and changes nothing; the message comes back after its timeout.
+  - `changeVisibility` works only on an in-flight message.
+  - A message already received `maxReceiveCount` times (`services.queue.max-receive-count`) moves to `deadLetters` on the next receive attempt instead of being delivered. This models the SQS redrive policy.
+  - Delivery is in send order. Long polls wait in real time, rechecking every 50 ms, and a send wakes them.
+  - Retention and SQS duplicate delivery are not modelled.
+- **Live semantics:**
+  - Every request carries `apiCallTimeout = services.timeouts.sqs-ms`; a receive adds its wait, because the HTTP call must outlive `WaitTimeSeconds`.
+  - The receive requests `ApproximateReceiveCount` and reads it; 1 when absent.
+  - `delete` returns false only for `ReceiptHandleIsInvalid`. SQS answers success for an old handle without deleting.
+  - `changeVisibility` returns false for `ReceiptHandleIsInvalid` or `MessageNotInflight`.
+  - Credentials: the shared `RefreshingCredentials` of the profile through `LiveAws.sqs`, with the expired-token refresh of `LiveAws.guarded`. SDK retries are off. No SQS call is retried here: none is a pure read (ruling 9), the worker's poll loop retries, and the job store makes a duplicate send or delivery harmless.
+- Phase 12 Terraform: a standard queue with visibility timeout 300 s, a redrive policy with maxReceiveCount 3 to the dead-letter queue (the same as configuration), and DLQ retention longer than the source queue's. The worker role needs `sqs:ReceiveMessage`, `sqs:DeleteMessage`, and `sqs:ChangeMessageVisibility`; the query-api role needs `sqs:SendMessage`.
+
+Client timeouts for the DynamoDB adapters come from the client: `LiveAws.dynamo(target, LiveAws.overridesWithTimeout(cfg.services.timeouts.dynamodbMs))`.
+
+## Window summaries (Phase 10 work order 8, follow-up F2)
+
+Code: `WindowSummaries.scala` (record, port, validator, converter), `cli/LoadWindowSummaryCli.scala`. Test: `WindowSummarySpec`. Input contract: Spark `corpus/window-summary`, `spark-corpus-window-summary-v1` (modules/spark/JOBS.md, "CorpusWindowSummaryJob").
+
+**Decision: one small S3 object, no DynamoDB table and no GSI.** It follows the coauthor-evidence pattern: a validated JSON object under `curated/`, written once and read through `ObjectReadPort`. Three rows (one per configured window) need no key lookup, so they stay in S3 (see "Kept in S3" above). Phase 12 needs no new table, index, or IAM action beyond `s3:GetObject` on the key for the role that runs the rag runtime.
+
+| Item | Value |
+|---|---|
+| Key | `S3Layout.windowSummary(snapshot, runId)` = `curated/window-summary/snapshot=<d>/run=<load run>/part-00000.jsonl` |
+| Run manifest | `runs/load-window-summary/snapshot=<d>/run=<load run>/manifest.json` (`store-run-manifest-v1`); `sourceRef` names the Spark table, Spark run, and the sha256 of its `_manifest.json` |
+| Load run ID | `StableIds.runId("load-window-summary", "load-window-summary-v1", configVersion, ["source=<spark run>", <sha256 of _manifest.json>], 1)` |
+| Line | one JSON object per window, ordered by start year, end year, window: exactly the 25 Spark columns (`WindowSummaries.Fields`, from `SparkSchema`), `sourceRuns` and `provenance` as nested objects, null IDs and fractions as JSON null |
+| Row provenance | `provenance.runId` is the Spark run that computed the row (general rule); the load run is in the key and the run manifest |
+
+Record and port (storage-local mirror of the Spark contract; no core record is needed):
+
+```scala
+final case class WindowSummarySourceRuns(temporalSummaryExportRunId: String, communityPathRunId: String,
+    researcherAnalyticsRunId: String, canonicalGraphRunId: String, inputHadoopRunId: String,
+    matchPolicyVersion: String)
+final case class WindowSummary(window: String, windowStartYear: Int, windowEndYear: Int,
+    researchers: Long, coauthorEdges: Long, sumJointPublicationCount: Long, components: Long,
+    largestComponentSize: Long, singletonResearchers: Long, communities: Long,
+    largestCommunitySize: Long, singletonCommunities: Long, communitiesAtLeastMinSize: Long,
+    largestComponentId: Option[String], largestCommunityId: Option[String], years: Int,
+    largestComponentFraction: Option[Double], largestCommunityFraction: Option[Double],
+    communityMinSize: Int, communityConverged: Boolean, communityRounds: Int, nodeBasis: String,
+    sourceRuns: WindowSummarySourceRuns, provenance: Provenance, schemaVersion: String)
+trait WindowSummaryReadPort:
+  def windowSummary(window: String): Option[WindowSummary]
+WindowSummaries.open(objects: ObjectReadPort, key: String, windows: Seq[TimeWindow])
+  : Either[Vector[Violation], WindowSummaryReadPort]
+```
+
+- **Open.** One `get` of the key, then `parse`; any violation refuses the whole object (nothing half-opened). The rows are then answered from memory. Fake: `FileObjectStore(root).reader`. Live: `S3ObjectStore(...).reader` (one GetObject, the existing adapter, no new live code). Both stores are write-once, so the copy never goes stale.
+- **Read.** `windowSummary(window)` returns the row or None for a name with no row. The benchmark alias `all` is not a stored name: the caller maps it to the first configured window (`all-years`), as BenchmarkPathJob does.
+- **Validation** (`WindowSummaries.parse`, codes): `BAD_KEY` (key rules, or not under `curated/window-summary/`); `MALFORMED` (not JSON, a field missing or extra, a wrong type), so long text or any other extra field is refused; `BAD_ROW` (schema version; a configured window with its years; `years`; counts non-negative and consistent; when researchers > 0, `comp-`/`comm-` IDs, largest sizes from 1 to researchers, and each fraction equal to size / researchers within 1e-12; when researchers = 0, no IDs, no fractions, sizes 0; `communityMinSize` at least 1; non-empty `nodeBasis`; `sourceRuns` run IDs and match-policy version; provenance `provenance-v1`, DERIVED, `sourceRecordId` = window, run ID, snapshot, a policy version, null model version); `DUPLICATE_WINDOW`; `MISSING_WINDOW` (every configured window must be present); `PROVENANCE` (rows from more than one run, or a key snapshot that differs from the rows'); `OBJECT_MISSING`.
+- **Conversion** (`WindowSummaries.fromSparkManifest`). Storage has no Parquet reader, so the loader reads the Spark run's `_manifest.json`. Its `counts.windowSummary` holds every column except `nodeBasis`, `sourceRuns`, and `provenance`. Those come from `settings.nodeBasis` and `sourceRuns`, and provenance is rebuilt as the job builds it (spark `Canonical.provenance`: `provenance-v1`, DERIVED, the window, the run's snapshot and run ID, `settings.graphPolicy`, null model). The manifest must have schema `spark-run-manifest-v1`, stage `spark-corpus-window-summary`, the `corpus/window-summary` table with exactly `SparkSchema`, `spark-corpus-window-summary-v1`, and a row count equal to the rows, and no integrity violation (`INTEGRITY_VIOLATIONS`); otherwise `BAD_MANIFEST`. A Spark column change therefore stops the loader. This is a shortcut until spark-engineer writes the store object directly.
+- **Idempotency.** The same Spark run gives the same load run ID and the same bytes, so a reload is `Unchanged`; different bytes under the key are a `Conflict`, are not written, and exit 1.
+
+**Phase 10 local store** (work order 8): from the work order 7 run `scratch/runtime/wo7/spark/snapshot=2026-10-03/run=run-1de2293597c338227290eb382de16957`, load run `run-6fbad498beb0d8f570df67665fb97544`, root `scratch/runtime/wo8/s3`:
+- object `curated/window-summary/snapshot=2026-10-03/run=run-6fbad498beb0d8f570df67665fb97544/part-00000.jsonl` (4,320 bytes, 3 rows);
+- manifest `runs/load-window-summary/snapshot=2026-10-03/run=run-6fbad498beb0d8f570df67665fb97544/manifest.json`.
+
+Wiring for the rag runtime: `WindowSummaries.open(FileObjectStore(Paths.get("scratch/runtime/wo8/s3")).reader, S3Layout.windowSummary("2026-10-03", "run-6fbad498beb0d8f570df67665fb97544"), cfg.graph.windows)`, once at startup; a `Left` stops startup. It is a separate object root from `scratch/stores/s3`, so it needs its own `FileObjectStore`. In the cloud the same call takes the `S3ObjectStore` reader and the same key.
+
 ## CLIs (`sbt "storage/runMain <class> ..."`)
 
 All CLIs run from the repository root, default to local fakes under `scratch/stores/`, and exit 0 on success, 1 on violations or failed work, 2 on a usage error, and 3 when free disk under the output directory is below 5 GiB (ruling 27; report BLOCKED-ON). Any uncaught Throwable, including a JVM error such as `OutOfMemoryError`, prints `<Cli> failed: ...` and exits 1 (`Cli.exitCode`, M1), so a failure never ends as sbt `[success]`. Store files are validated in full before anything is written.

+  test("neighbors pages: fixed order, pages concatenate to the whole walk, offset bounds") {
+    withGraph { (graph, _) =>
+      val r = graph.reader(limits)
+      val types = Seq("COAUTHORED_WITH", "AUTHORED")
+      val all = r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 50, types)
+      assert(all.nodes.size >= 4 && !all.truncated)
+      assert(r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 50, types) == all) // same store, same order
+      val pages =
+        all.nodes.indices.map(k => r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 1, types, offset = k))
+      assert(pages.flatMap(_.nodes) == all.nodes)
+      assert(pages.init.forall(_.truncated) && !pages.last.truncated)
+      assert(pages.flatMap(_.edges).map(_.id).toSet == all.edges.map(_.id).toSet)
+      pages.foreach(p =>
+        assert(p.edges.forall(e => p.nodes.exists(n => n.id == e.from || n.id == e.to)))
+      )
+      val two = r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 2, types, offset = 2)
+      assert(two.nodes == all.nodes.slice(2, 4))
+      assert(r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 5, types, offset = all.nodes.size).nodes.isEmpty)
+      assertThrows[IllegalArgumentException](
+        r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 5, types, offset = -1)
+      )
+      assertThrows[IllegalArgumentException](
+        r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 5, types, offset = limits.maxResults - 4)
+      )
+      assert(
+        r.neighbors(a, Direction.BOTH, 2, 5, types, offset = limits.maxResults - 5).nodes.isEmpty
+      )
+    }

### modules/rag/src/main/scala/scholarchain/rag/runtime/RuntimeApi.scala
```
     1	package scholarchain.rag.runtime
     2	
     3	import scholarchain.core.{EvidenceItem, IdFormat}
     4	import scholarchain.storage.{
     5	  AttributeValue,
     6	  Hashing,
     7	  Item,
     8	  KeyValueReadPort,
     9	  ObjectReadPort,
    10	  TableName
    11	}
    12	import upickle.default.{read, ReadWriter}
    13	
    14	import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8
    15	import scala.util.Try
    16	
    17	/** The service-facing runtime API (Phase 10 work order 1): plain Scala, no HTTP. Routes call these
    18	  * and add no retrieval, verification, or store logic of their own (Phase 10 ruling 3).
    19	  */
    20	object RuntimeApi:
    21	
    22	  /** Answers one structured request (the POST /v1/query fields as a QueryRequest) with the given
    23	    * ports, under a deadline of deadlineMs from now. The result holds the answer, the audit record,
    24	    * the evidence bundle (None when the answer abstained before building one), and deadlinePassed.
    25	    * Left is a stage failure (a store error, a refused write, or a malformed question or query ID).
    26	    */
    27	  def answer(
    28	      request: QueryRequest,
    29	      ports: RuntimePorts,
    30	      setup: RuntimeSetup,
    31	      deadlineMs: Long
    32	  ): Either[RuntimeFailure, RuntimeResult] =
    33	    Workflow.answer(request, ports, setup, Some(deadlineMs))
    34	
    35	  private def readObject[A: ReadWriter](
    36	      objects: ObjectReadPort,
    37	      key: String
    38	  ): Either[String, Option[A]] =
    39	    objects.get(key) match
    40	      case None        => Right(None)
    41	      case Some(bytes) =>
    42	        Try(read[A](new String(bytes, UTF_8))).toEither.left
    43	          .map(e => s"$key does not decode: ${e.getClass.getSimpleName}")
    44	          .map(Some(_))
    45	
    46	  /** The audit record of a query of this runtime run (setup snapshot and run ID). Right(None) when
    47	    * there is none, or when queryId is not qry-<32 hex> (no key is built from other text).
    48	    */
    49	  def audit(
    50	      queryId: String,
    51	      setup: RuntimeSetup,
    52	      audits: ObjectReadPort
    53	  ): Either[String, Option[AuditRecord]] =
    54	    if !IdFormat.isQueryId(queryId) then Right(None)
    55	    else readObject[AuditRecord](audits, Audit.auditKey(setup.snapshot, setup.runId, queryId))
    56	
    57	  /** The stored answer of a query of this runtime run; Right(None) as for audit. */
    58	  def storedAnswer(
    59	      queryId: String,
    60	      setup: RuntimeSetup,
    61	      audits: ObjectReadPort
    62	  ): Either[String, Option[RuntimeAnswer]] =
    63	    if !IdFormat.isQueryId(queryId) then Right(None)
    64	    else readObject[RuntimeAnswer](audits, Audit.answerKey(setup.snapshot, setup.runId, queryId))
    65	
    66	  /** The audit without model text (Phase 10 ruling 8): removed claims and left-out lines keep their
    67	    * IDs, statuses, checks, and reasons, never their text.
    68	    */
    69	  def withoutModelText(a: AuditRecord): AuditRecord =
    70	    a.copy(
    71	      removedClaims = a.removedClaims.map(_.copy(text = "")),
    72	      droppedLines = a.droppedLines.map(_.copy(text = ""))
    73	    )
    74	
    75	  /** Whether the answer used no language model (fake or stub mode): only then is the plan from the
    76	    * rule planner (PlannerSource.RULES); a live model run plans MODEL or FALLBACK_RULES.
    77	    */
    78	  def llmStubbed(a: AuditRecord): Boolean = a.plan.source == PlannerSource.RULES
    79	
    80	  /** One EvidenceBundle EV# item as the core EvidenceItem: the inline payload, or the payloadRef
    81	    * object, whose sha256 must equal payloadHash.
    82	    */
    83	  def evidenceOf(item: Item, objects: ObjectReadPort): Either[String, EvidenceItem] =
    84	    def s(k: String) = item.get(k).collect { case AttributeValue.S(v) => v }
    85	    val text = (s("payload"), s("payloadRef")) match
    86	      case (Some(p), None)   => Right(p)
    87	      case (None, Some(ref)) =>
    88	        objects.get(ref) match
    89	          case None                                                    => Left(s"$ref is missing")
    90	          case Some(b) if s("payloadHash").contains(Hashing.sha256(b)) =>
    91	            Right(new String(b, UTF_8))
    92	          case Some(_) => Left(s"$ref does not match its payloadHash")
    93	      case _ => Left("the item has neither one payload nor one payloadRef")
    94	    text.flatMap(t =>
    95	      Try(read[EvidenceItem](t)).toEither.left.map(e =>
    96	        s"payload does not decode: ${e.getClass.getSimpleName}"
    97	      )
    98	    )
    99	
   100	  /** An evidence item of a query's bundle (EvidenceBundle table, key queryId and EV#<evidenceId>).
   101	    * Right(None) when absent or when either ID is malformed. A lookup by evidence ID alone needs a
   102	    * storage index (reported to storage-engineer, Phase 10 work order 4).
   103	    */
   104	  def evidence(
   105	      queryId: String,
   106	      evidenceId: String,
   107	      records: KeyValueReadPort,
   108	      objects: ObjectReadPort
   109	  ): Either[String, Option[EvidenceItem]] =
   110	    if !IdFormat.isQueryId(queryId) || !IdFormat.isEvidenceId(evidenceId) then Right(None)
   111	    else
   112	      records
   113	        .get(TableName.EvidenceBundle, queryId, s"EV#$evidenceId")
   114	        .fold[Either[String, Option[EvidenceItem]]](Right(None))(i =>
   115	          evidenceOf(i, objects).map(Some(_))
   116	        )
```

   168	          VerificationStatus.UNSUPPORTED
   169	      )
   170	      assert(
   171	        Verifier
   172	          .check(
   173	            d(
   174	              "In 2000-2014, 38 communities have size 3 or more.",
   175	              right + ("communitiesAtLeastMinSize" -> "38")
   176	            ),
   177	            c
   178	          )
   179	          .status == VerificationStatus.CONFLICTING
   180	      )
   181	    }

   193	  test("AskCli: --window-summary-root and --window-summary-run wire the port; bad use is refused") {
   194	    val root = tempDir()
   195	    try
   196	      val seg = s"snapshot=$snapshot/run=$graphRun"
   197	      writeGraph(root.resolve(s"graph/$seg"))
   198	      writeItems(root.resolve(s"items/$seg"))
   199	      writeVectors(root.resolve("vectors"))
   200	      FileObjectStore(root.resolve("s3")).loader.putNew(profileRef, profileText.getBytes(UTF_8))
   201	      writeSummaries(root.resolve("ws"))
   202	      val base = Vector(
   203	        "--graph",
   204	        root.resolve(s"graph/$seg").toString,
   205	        "--items",
   206	        root.resolve(s"items/$seg").toString,
   207	        "--objects",
   208	        root.resolve("s3").toString,
   209	        "--vectors",
   210	        root.resolve("vectors").toString,
   211	        "--out",
   212	        root.resolve("out").toString,
   213	        "--question",
   214	        b13
   215	      )
   216	      def run(extra: String*): (Int, Vector[String]) =
   217	        val lines = Vector.newBuilder[String]
   218	        val code = AskCli.run(base ++ extra, lines += _, _ => (), () => 0L, env)
   219	        (code, lines.result())
   220	      val (code, out) =
   221	        run(
   222	          "--window-summary-root",
   223	          root.resolve("ws").toString,
   224	          "--window-summary-run",
   225	          summaryLoadRun
   226	        )
   227	      assert(code == 0, out.mkString("\n"))
   228	      assert(out.exists(_.contains(s"window summaries: $summaryKey")), out.mkString("\n"))
   229	      assert(out.exists(l => l.contains("[VERIFIED]") && l.contains("33 connected components")))
   230	      assert(run("--window-summary-root", root.resolve("ws").toString)._1 == 2)
   231	      assert(run("--window-summary-run", summaryLoadRun)._1 == 2)
   232	      assert(
   233	        run(
   234	          "--window-summary-root",
   235	          root.resolve("ws").toString,
   236	          "--window-summary-run",
   237	          "x"
   238	        )._1 == 2
   239	      )
   240	      // An object that is not there (another load run) refuses the stores: exit 1.
   241	      val other = summaryRun
   242	      val (missing, lines) =
   243	        run("--window-summary-root", root.resolve("ws").toString, "--window-summary-run", other)
   244	      assert(missing == 1 && lines.exists(_.contains("OBJECT_MISSING")), lines.mkString("\n"))
   245	    finally deleteTree(root)
   246	  }

### modules/rag/src/test/scala/scholarchain/rag/runtime/RuntimeApiSpec.scala
```
     1	package scholarchain.rag.runtime
     2	
     3	import org.scalatest.funsuite.AnyFunSuite
     4	import scholarchain.core.{EvidenceItem, StableIds}
     5	import scholarchain.storage.{AttributeValue, Hashing}
     6	import upickle.default.write
     7	
     8	import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8
     9	
    10	/** The service-facing API (Phase 10 work order 1): one structured request under a deadline, and the
    11	  * audit, answer, and evidence lookups, on the fixture world (no network).
    12	  */
    13	class RuntimeApiSpec extends AnyFunSuite:
    14	  import RuntimeFixtures.*
    15	
    16	  private val q = "Have Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper coauthored a paper?"
    17	  private def request(question: String) =
    18	    QueryRequest(RuntimeSchemas.QueryRequest, "", Some("s-1"), question, None, None, None, None)
    19	
    20	  test("answer: one request under a deadline gives the answer, the audit, and the bundle") {
    21	    withWorld() { w =>
    22	      val r =
    23	        RuntimeApi.answer(request(q), w.ports, w.setup, 60000L).fold(f => fail(s"$f"), identity)
    24	      assert(!r.deadlinePassed && r.answer.abstention.isEmpty && r.bundle.isDefined)
    25	      assert(r.audit.queryId == r.answer.response.queryId)
    26	      assert(r.answer.response.claims.exists(_.verificationStatus.toString == "VERIFIED"))
    27	      // Structured fields keep overriding the model (ruling 4): a request window is applied.
    28	      val ranged = request(q).copy(constraints =
    29	        Some(RequestConstraints(Some(2000), Some(2014), None, None, None))
    30	      )
    31	      val rr = RuntimeApi.answer(ranged, w.ports, w.setup, 60000L).fold(f => fail(s"$f"), identity)
    32	      assert(rr.answer.plan.window.window == "2000-2014")
    33	      assert(rr.answer.plan.overriddenFields.nonEmpty)
    34	    }
    35	  }
    36	
    37	  test("answer: a passed deadline abstains and is flagged; the audit is still written") {
    38	    withWorld() { w =>
    39	      // The fixture clock advances 1 ms per reading, so a 3 ms budget passes inside the workflow.
    40	      val r = RuntimeApi.answer(request(q), w.ports, w.setup, 3L).fold(f => fail(s"$f"), identity)
    41	      assert(r.deadlinePassed)
    42	      assert(r.answer.abstention.map(_.reason).contains(AbstentionReason.BUDGET_EXHAUSTED))
    43	      assert(r.answer.abstention.exists(_.detail.contains("3 ms")))
    44	      assert(RuntimeApi.audit(r.audit.queryId, w.setup, w.audit.reader) == Right(Some(r.audit)))
    45	    }
    46	  }
    47	
    48	  test("lookups: audit and answer by query ID, evidence by query and evidence ID") {
    49	    withWorld() { w =>
    50	      val r =
    51	        RuntimeApi.answer(request(q), w.ports, w.setup, 60000L).fold(f => fail(s"$f"), identity)
    52	      val id = r.audit.queryId
    53	      assert(RuntimeApi.audit(id, w.setup, w.audit.reader) == Right(Some(r.audit)))
    54	      assert(RuntimeApi.storedAnswer(id, w.setup, w.audit.reader) == Right(Some(r.answer)))
    55	      assert(RuntimeApi.llmStubbed(r.audit)) // fake mode: the rule planner, no model
    56	      val unknown = StableIds.queryId(w.setup.runId, "x", "y", 1)
    57	      assert(RuntimeApi.audit(unknown, w.setup, w.audit.reader) == Right(None))
    58	      // A malformed ID never becomes a store key.
    59	      assert(RuntimeApi.audit("../../etc", w.setup, w.audit.reader) == Right(None))
    60	      val item = r.bundle.get.graphEvidence.head
    61	      assert(
    62	        RuntimeApi.evidence(id, item.evidenceId, w.records.reader, w.audit.reader) ==
    63	          Right(Some(item))
    64	      )
    65	      val cited = r.answer.response.claims.flatMap(_.evidenceIds).distinct
    66	      assert(cited.nonEmpty)
    67	      cited.foreach(e =>
    68	        assert(RuntimeApi.evidence(id, e, w.records.reader, w.audit.reader).exists(_.isDefined), e)
    69	      )
    70	      val other = StableIds.evidenceId(item.evidenceType, Seq("nothing"), graphRun)
    71	      assert(RuntimeApi.evidence(id, other, w.records.reader, w.audit.reader) == Right(None))
    72	      assert(RuntimeApi.evidence(id, "ev-zz", w.records.reader, w.audit.reader) == Right(None))
    73	    }
    74	  }
    75	
    76	  test("evidenceOf: a payloadRef object must match its payloadHash") {
    77	    withWorld() { w =>
    78	      val r =
    79	        RuntimeApi.answer(request(q), w.ports, w.setup, 60000L).fold(f => fail(s"$f"), identity)
    80	      val e: EvidenceItem = r.bundle.get.graphEvidence.head
    81	      val bytes = write(e).getBytes(UTF_8)
    82	      val key = Audit.evidenceKey(w.setup.snapshot, w.setup.runId, "qry-test", e.evidenceId)
    83	      w.audit.loader.putNew(key, bytes)
    84	      def item(hash: String) = Map(
    85	        "payloadRef" -> AttributeValue.S(key),
    86	        "payloadHash" -> AttributeValue.S(hash)
    87	      )
    88	      assert(RuntimeApi.evidenceOf(item(Hashing.sha256(bytes)), w.audit.reader) == Right(e))
    89	      assert(
    90	        RuntimeApi.evidenceOf(item(Hashing.sha256("x".getBytes(UTF_8))), w.audit.reader).isLeft
    91	      )
    92	      assert(RuntimeApi.evidenceOf(Map.empty, w.audit.reader).isLeft)
    93	    }
    94	  }

### modules/services/SERVICES.md [CHANGED BY repair-01 Part A]
```
     1	# ScholarChain services: pools, S3 access, and the runtime run ID
     2	
     3	Owner: service-engineer. This file covers the deployment facts of `modules/services` that the code alone does not state. Routes, status codes, and bodies are in `openapi.yaml`. Configuration keys and their defaults are in `modules/core/src/main/resources/scholarchain.conf` (`services { ... }`). Phase 10 repair-01 wrote this file for code review 1 findings F1, m1, m2, and m3.
     4	
     5	## 1. Deployables
     6	
     7	| Task | Main class | Port key (default) | Answers questions |
     8	|---|---|---|---|
     9	| query-api | `scholarchain.services.main.QueryApi` | `services.query-api.port` (8080) | yes: sync `POST /v1/query` runs the rag workflow |
    10	| graph-evidence-api | `scholarchain.services.main.GraphEvidenceApi` | `services.graph-evidence-api.port` (8081) | no |
    11	| worker | `scholarchain.services.main.Worker` | `services.worker.port` (8082) | yes: it runs async jobs |
    12	
    13	`scholarchain.services.main.LocalRun` starts all three in one JVM in fake mode on 127.0.0.1 (runMain only).
    14	
    15	## 2. Thread pools (F1)
    16	
    17	Every downstream call goes through `Downstreams` (`Resilience.scala`). Each call has a timeout and a circuit breaker per downstream. Each service has two downstream pools, so long workflows never take the threads that store calls and readiness probes need. No configuration key was added. The pool sizes come from existing keys:
    18	
    19	| Service | HTTP threads | Store pool (store calls and readiness probes) | Workflow pool (no queue) |
    20	|---|---|---|---|
    21	| query-api | `services.query-api.threads` = T (8) | T threads, queue `services.max-queued-requests` (64) | `max(1, T / 2)` slots (4): the sync workflow (`RuntimeApi.answer`) |
    22	| graph-evidence-api | `services.graph-evidence-api.threads` = G (16) | G threads, queue 64 | 1 slot, never used: it runs no workflow, and no thread is started for it |
    23	| worker | `services.worker.threads` = W (2) | `services.worker.concurrency` + W = C + W threads (3), queue 64 | C slots (1): the job workflow |
    24	
    25	Which calls run on which pool:
    26	
    27	- **query-api store pool:** the idempotency claim, job create, read, and transition (dynamodb), the SQS send, the S3 reads of explain, result, and replay, and the three readiness probes (s3, dynamodb, neptune).
    28	- **query-api workflow pool:** the sync workflow only.
    29	- **graph-evidence-api store pool:** the evidence lookup (dynamodb), the evidence payload read (s3), the node and neighbor reads (neptune), and the two readiness probes (neptune, dynamodb).
    30	- **worker store pool:** the SQS receive, delete, and the job reads and transitions (dynamodb), and the three readiness probes (dynamodb, s3, neptune).
    31	- **worker workflow pool:** the job workflow only.
    32	
    33	Why these sizes:
    34	
    35	- Each HTTP thread (and each worker poll loop) holds at most one store call at a time. A store pool as large as its callers therefore does not queue in normal operation.
    36	- A sync request holds its HTTP thread while its workflow runs. With at most T / 2 workflow slots, at least T - T / 2 HTTP threads stay free for the job, result, explain, and readiness routes. With T = 1 no thread is reserved. The default is T = 8.
    37	- The worker runs at most C jobs at once (the model quota, ruling 10), so it has C workflow slots.
    38	
    39	Two rules apply to every call:
    40	
    41	1. **A call's timeout counts from its start, not from its submission.** A call that waited for a thread still gets its full timeout once it runs.
    42	2. **A call that cannot start answers 503 OVERLOADED and touches no breaker.** This covers a full workflow pool (refused at once, because there is no queue) and a store call still waiting for a thread when its timeout passes (given up, and never run). Waiting for a thread says nothing about the health of the downstream.
    43	
    44	Only a call that started and then timed out (`DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT`) or failed (`DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE`) counts toward its breaker. That rule is unchanged from work order 5.
    45	
    46	## 3. Behaviour under saturation
    47	
    48	This replaces the work order 5 risk text, "sustained slow model calls can therefore give 503 OVERLOADED". Code review 1 showed that text did not match the code then: queued calls timed out as DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT and opened breakers against healthy stores. The behaviour now:
    49	
    50	- **Every workflow slot of query-api is held.** The slots may be held by sync workflows still running, or by workflows past their 504 hard timeout. Those run on to their cooperative deadline, which a model call can overrun by up to `runtime.llm.timeout-ms`.
    51	  - A new sync `POST /v1/query` answers 503 OVERLOADED (retryable) at once and touches no breaker.
    52	  - Async POSTs, `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}`, `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result`, `GET /v1/explain/{queryId}`, idempotent replays, and `/readyz` keep their own threads. They answer within their store timeouts.
    53	  - `/readyz` stays ready, and no breaker opens.
    54	  - Tested: `SaturationSpec` "query-api: with every workflow slot held ...".
    55	- **Every workflow slot of the worker is held** (a job past its backstop, `runtime.question.deadline-ms` + `runtime.llm.timeout-ms`).
    56	  - The poll loop does not receive.
    57	  - A message already received is not claimed. Its job stays QUEUED, and the message comes back after the visibility timeout.
    58	  - Readiness and job-state writes keep their threads, and no breaker opens.
    59	  - Tested: `SaturationSpec` "worker: ...".
    60	- **The store pool is full.** This happens only when store calls overrun their timeouts and ignore the interrupt.
    61	  - A store call still waiting when its timeout passes answers 503 OVERLOADED, and no breaker counts it.
    62	  - A readiness probe that cannot run makes `/readyz` report NOT_READY for that downstream while the pool is full. No breaker opens, so readiness returns as soon as threads free.
    63	  - Tested: `BuildingBlocksSpec` "waiting to start is no downstream failure ...".
    64	- **The HTTP pool is full** (T busy threads plus 64 queued requests). The service answers 503 OVERLOADED, as in work order 5.
    65	
    66	## 4. S3 access per task (m1, m3)
    67	
    68	S3 answers a GET of a missing key with 404 (NoSuchKey) only when the caller has `s3:ListBucket` on the bucket. Without it, S3 answers 403 (AccessDenied). The storage adapter maps only NoSuchKey to "not found" (`S3ObjectStore`). A 403 is a downstream failure (502) that counts toward the s3 breaker.
    69	
    70	Two code paths read keys that usually do not exist:
    71	
    72	- **The readiness probe.** query-api and the worker read `logs/rag-runtime/snapshot=<d>/run=<runtime run ID>/readiness-probe`. This key lies under the audit prefix those tasks already read and write (`Probes.objectKey`). It was at the bucket root before repair-01.
    73	  - Without `s3:ListBucket`, `/readyz` is NOT_READY permanently.
    74	- **Unknown query IDs.** explain reads `logs/rag-runtime/.../<queryId>.json`. Result and replay read `reports/rag-runtime/.../<queryId>.answer.json`.
    75	  - Without `s3:ListBucket`, a well-formed but unknown `qry-` ID answers 502 instead of 404, and it counts toward the s3 breaker.
    76	
    77	What each task needs, for the object reads and writes that the services wire. The runtime's retrieval reads, for example `curated/coauthor-evidence/*` and `semantic-context/text/*`, are listed in `modules/rag/RUNTIME.md` and `modules/storage/STORAGE.md`.
    78	
    79	| Task | `s3:GetObject` | `s3:PutObject` | `s3:ListBucket` on the data bucket |
    80	|---|---|---|---|
    81	| query-api | `logs/rag-runtime/*` (audits, evidence payloads, the readiness probe); `reports/rag-runtime/*` (stored answers); `curated/window-summary/*` (once at startup, when `runtime.window-summary.load-run` is set); the runtime retrieval prefixes | `logs/rag-runtime/*`, `reports/rag-runtime/*` (sync answers; write-once) | **yes**. It may be limited with an `s3:prefix` condition to `logs/rag-runtime/` and `reports/rag-runtime/`. |
    82	| worker | the same as query-api, including `curated/window-summary/*` | the same as query-api (async answers) | **yes**, as for query-api (readiness probe, and the read-back of a write-once object) |
    83	| graph-evidence-api | `logs/rag-runtime/*` (evidence payloads referenced by `payloadRef`) | none | not needed by its code paths. It has no S3 readiness probe, and it reads only keys that an EvidenceBundle item names. |
    84	
    85	**Window summaries (m3).** Only query-api and the worker open the window-summary object. graph-evidence-api answers no questions, so `GraphEvidenceApi` starts with `answers = false` and never opens it. It needs no `curated/window-summary/*` grant.
    86	- It still takes the key as a runtime run ID input, so its run ID equals that of query-api. In fake mode, the evidence records directory is named by that run ID.
    87	- With `SCHOLARCHAIN_WINDOW_SUMMARY_RUN` set, it prints `window summaries: <key> not opened (this service answers no questions)`.
    88	- Tested: `CorpusQuerySpec` "graph-evidence-api never opens the window-summary object ...".
    89	
    90	## 5. The runtime run ID: deploy query-api and the worker together (m2)
    91	
    92	The async result path works only when query-api and the worker compute the **same runtime run ID**. The reasons:
    93	- The worker writes the answer and the audit under `reports/rag-runtime/...` and `logs/rag-runtime/...` of its own run ID.
    94	- query-api reads `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result` and `GET /v1/explain/{queryId}` under its own run ID.
    95	- No result reference is stored (ruling 16).
    96	
    97	If the run IDs differ, a SUCCEEDED job's result answers 502, and its explain answers 404.
    98	
    99	The run ID is `Workflow.runIdFor` over these inputs. Both tasks must have the same value for every one:
   100	
   101	| Input | Set by |
   102	|---|---|
   103	| code version and verifier version | the build (the same image) |
   104	| configuration version | the same configuration file and environment overrides |
   105	| graph, items, and vectors runs | live: `--snapshot`, `--graph-run`, `--items-run`, `--vectors-run`; fake: the `--graph` and `--items` path segments and the vector file contents |
   106	| embedding model version | `models.embedding-model` (`SCHOLARCHAIN_EMBEDDING_MODEL_ID`) |
   107	| window-summary key | `--snapshot` plus `runtime.window-summary.load-run` (`SCHOLARCHAIN_WINDOW_SUMMARY_RUN`) |
   108	| prompt versions | `runtime.planner.prompt-version`, `runtime.synthesis.prompt-version`, and the prompt files |
   109	| planner and synthesis model IDs | `models.planner-model`, `models.synthesis-model` |
   110	| mode | `--live` or fake |
   111	
   112	Deployment rules (Phase 12 carry-forward, ruling 23):
   113	
   114	- Deploy query-api and the worker together, from one image and one task-definition revision of arguments and environment. A rolling deploy in which one task serves a different revision breaks results for jobs in flight.
   115	- graph-evidence-api should use the same inputs too. In live mode its evidence lookup does not depend on the run ID (it uses the evidenceById index).
   116	
   117	Each service prints its runtime run ID once at startup, on standard output. That line already existed and is unchanged:
   118	
   119	- `live mode: runtime run <run ID>, snapshot <date>, model calls stubbed|live`
   120	- `fake mode: runtime run <run ID>, snapshot <date>, outputs under <dir>`
   121	
   122	Compare the line of the query-api task with that of the worker task after every deployment.
```

### modules/services/openapi.yaml [CHANGED BY repair-01 Part A]
```
     1	openapi: 3.0.3
     2	info:
     3	  title: ScholarChain REST services
     4	  version: v1
     5	  description: |
     6	    Three deployables built from modules/services (Phase 10 ruling 1):
     7	    query-api (POST /v1/query, GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}, GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result,
     8	    GET /v1/explain/{queryId}), graph-evidence-api (GET /v1/evidence/{evidenceId},
     9	    GET /v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}, GET /v1/graph/nodes/{nodeId}/neighbors), and worker (an SQS
    10	    consumer with no API routes). Every service also serves GET /healthz, GET /readyz, and GET /metrics.
    11	
    12	    Async jobs (ruling 7): POST /v1/query with "execution": "async" writes a QUEUED job record and
    13	    sends one queue message holding only the job and query IDs. The worker moves the job
    14	    QUEUED -> RUNNING -> SUCCEEDED or FAILED with conditional writes (only the worker whose
    15	    QUEUED -> RUNNING write passed runs it, so a redelivered message never runs a job twice), runs
    16	    it under runtime.question.deadline-ms, and deletes the message only after a final state is
    17	    written. A job still RUNNING at its message's last delivery (services.queue.max-receive-count)
    18	    is moved to FAILED (DEADLINE_EXCEEDED). Malformed messages, and messages whose job does not
    19	    exist, are never run or deleted: the queue's redrive policy moves them to the dead-letter queue.
    20	
    21	    Conventions:
    22	    - JSON only (Content-Type application/json on POST), path versioning /v1.
    23	    - Every response carries X-Request-Id and X-Trace-Id. A client value of 1 to 128 characters of
    24	      [A-Za-z0-9._:-] is echoed; otherwise one is generated (req-<32 hex>, trace-<32 hex>).
    25	    - Every error body is ErrorBody: a typed error code, message, traceId, requestId, retryable.
    26	    - Authentication: one API key in X-Api-Key, compared in constant time with the value of the
    27	      environment variable named by services.api-key-env. Missing or wrong key: 401. /healthz and
    28	      /readyz need no key. 403 FORBIDDEN is reserved and unused in the single-key deployment.
    29	    - Malformed IDs are answered 404 without a store read.
    30	    - Option fields of response bodies are always present, null when absent.
    31	
    32	    Every example under openapi/examples/ was produced by the fake-mode test fixture world
    33	    (ServiceFixtures, three fictional researchers), except the two query-request examples, which are
    34	    hand-written (the first is the spec request). They are not results of the deployed corpus.
    35	    ServicesContractSpec parses every example against the Scala models.

    49	      description: |
    50	        Validates the body, mints a query ID, and claims the Idempotency-Key when one is sent.
    51	        "execution": "sync" (default) runs the Phase 9 workflow inline under
    52	        operations.synchronous-deadline-ms (a hard timeout at the same value backs the runtime's
    53	        cooperative deadline) and answers 200. "execution": "async" writes a QUEUED job and one queue
    54	        message and answers 202 with the job reference; if the message cannot be sent the job is
    55	        moved to FAILED at once and the answer is 502. Structured fields override the planner
    56	        (Phase 9 ruling 4). Rate limited per instance by services.query-api.query-rate-per-minute
    57	        (sync and async alike).
    58	        With an Idempotency-Key: the same key and canonical body replays the stored response
    59	        (header Idempotent-Replay: true): the job reference (202, with the job's current state) when
    60	        the first request was async, otherwise the stored answer (200). A different body (including
    61	        a different execution) is 409; a claimed sync key whose answer is not stored (still running,
    62	        or failed) is 409. Without a key every POST is a new query.

   379	        order of the storage port (level by level, discovery order) and concatenate to the whole
   380	        walk; at most graph-read.max-results nodes in all. nextCursor is an opaque cursor bound to
   381	        (node, direction, depth, edge types); it is present when truncated and a full page was
   382	        returned. The cursor is recomputed from the start, so pages are consistent while the graph
   383	        does not change.
   384	      parameters:
   385	        - $ref: '#/components/parameters/NodeId'
   386	        - name: direction
   387	          in: query
   388	          required: true
   389	          schema:
   390	            type: string
   391	            enum: [in, out, both]
   392	        - name: depth
   393	          in: query
   394	          required: true
   395	          schema:
   396	            type: integer
   397	            minimum: 1
   398	            maximum: 3
   399	          description: 1 to graph-read.neighbors-max-depth (3)
   400	        - name: limit
   401	          in: query
   402	          required: true
   403	          schema:
   404	            type: integer
   405	            minimum: 1
   406	            maximum: 200
   407	          description: 1 to graph-read.neighbors-max-limit (200)
   408	        - name: edgeTypes
   409	          in: query
   410	          required: true
   411	          description: Comma-separated, non-empty subset of the storage edge labels (NeptuneContract.Edges), a set (order and repeats ignored)
   412	          schema:
   413	            type: string
   414	            example: COAUTHORED_WITH,AUTHORED,HAS_TOPIC,AFFILIATED_WITH,PUBLISHED_IN
   415	        - name: cursor
   416	          in: query
   417	          required: false
   418	          schema:
   419	            type: string
   420	        - $ref: '#/components/parameters/RequestId'
   421	        - $ref: '#/components/parameters/TraceId'
   422	      responses:
   423	        '200':
   424	          description: One page of nodes and the traversed edges touching them
   425	          content:
   426	            application/json:
   427	              schema:
   428	                $ref: '#/components/schemas/NeighborsResponse'
   429	              examples:
   430	                page:
   431	                  externalValue: openapi/examples/neighbors-response.json
   432	        '400':
   433	          description: INVALID_FIELD (a parameter missing, repeated, unknown, or out of bounds; a cursor of another walk)
   434	          content:
   435	            application/json:
   436	              schema:
   437	                $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   438	              examples:
   439	                depth:
   440	                  externalValue: openapi/examples/error-invalid-field.json
   441	        '401':
   442	          $ref: '#/components/responses/Unauthorized'
   443	        '404':
   444	          $ref: '#/components/responses/NodeNotFound'
   445	        '502':
   446	          $ref: '#/components/responses/DownstreamFailure'
   447	        '503':
   448	          $ref: '#/components/responses/Unavailable'
   449	        '504':
   450	          $ref: '#/components/responses/Timeout'
   451	  /healthz:
   452	    get:
   453	      summary: Liveness (every service); no key; depends on no downstream
   454	      security: []
   455	      responses:
   456	        '200':
   457	          description: The process serves requests
   458	          content:
   459	            application/json:
   460	              schema:
   461	                $ref: '#/components/schemas/Status'
   462	              examples:
   463	                ok:
   464	                  externalValue: openapi/examples/status-healthz.json
   465	        '503':
   466	          $ref: '#/components/responses/Unavailable'
   467	  /readyz:
   468	    get:
   469	      summary: Readiness (every service); no key
   470	      description: Probe reads of each downstream the service uses (a key or ID no store holds) through the same timeouts and breakers, and no open breaker. The 503 message names downstreams only (s3, dynamodb, neptune, runtime), never endpoints or configuration.
   471	      security: []
   472	      responses:
   473	        '200':
   474	          description: Ready
   475	          content:
   476	            application/json:
   477	              schema:
   478	                $ref: '#/components/schemas/Status'
   479	              examples:
   480	                ready:
   481	                  externalValue: openapi/examples/status-readyz.json
   482	        '503':
   483	          description: NOT_READY
   484	          content:
   485	            application/json:
   486	              schema:
   487	                $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   488	              examples:
   489	                notReady:
   490	                  externalValue: openapi/examples/error-not-ready.json
   491	  /metrics:
   492	    get:
   493	      summary: In-process counters (every service; API key required)
   494	      description: Request counts by route template and status, latency percentiles over the last 1024 requests per route, and counters (timeouts, answers, abstentions, model requests and tokens, verification status counts, queueReceives, jobsQueued, jobsSucceeded, jobsFailed, duplicateDeliveries, poisonMessages, stuckJobsFailed, circuitOpened.<downstream>). Each service counts its own work (the worker counts receives and jobs). Present when services.metrics-enabled is true.
   495	      responses:
   496	        '200':
   497	          description: Counters
   498	          content:
   499	            application/json:
   500	              schema:
   501	                type: object
   502	                required: [requests, latencyMs, counters]
   503	              examples:
   504	                counters:
   505	                  externalValue: openapi/examples/metrics-response.json
   506	        '401':
   507	          $ref: '#/components/responses/Unauthorized'
   508	components:
   509	  securitySchemes:
   510	    apiKey:
   511	      type: apiKey
   512	      in: header
   513	      name: X-Api-Key
   514	  parameters:
   515	    IdempotencyKey:
   516	      name: Idempotency-Key
   517	      in: header
   518	      required: false
   519	      description: 1 to 255 visible ASCII characters; stored hashed; expires after services.ttl.idempotency-hours
   520	      schema:
   521	        type: string
   522	    RequestId:
   523	      name: X-Request-Id
   524	      in: header
   525	      required: false
   526	      schema:
   527	        type: string
   528	        pattern: '^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$'
   529	    TraceId:
   530	      name: X-Trace-Id
   531	      in: header
   532	      required: false
   533	      schema:
   534	        type: string
   535	        pattern: '^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$'
   536	    JobId:
   537	      name: jobId
   538	      in: path
   539	      required: true
   540	      description: job-<32 hex> (IdFormat.isJobId); a malformed ID is 404 without a store read
   541	      schema:
   542	        type: string
   543	        pattern: '^job-[0-9a-f]{32}$'
   544	    NodeId:
   545	      name: nodeId
   546	      in: path
   547	      required: true
   548	      description: A stable node ID (IdFormat.isNodeId), percent-encoded
   549	      schema:
   550	        type: string
   551	  responses:
   552	    BadRequest:
   553	      description: INVALID_FIELD (for example a query parameter on a route that takes none)
   554	      content:
   555	        application/json:
   556	          schema:
   557	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   558	    Unauthorized:
   559	      description: UNAUTHORIZED (missing or wrong X-Api-Key)
   560	      content:
   561	        application/json:
   562	          schema:
   563	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   564	          examples:
   565	            unauthorized:
   566	              externalValue: openapi/examples/error-unauthorized.json
   567	    JobNotFound:
   568	      description: JOB_NOT_FOUND (also for a malformed or expired ID)
   569	      content:
   570	        application/json:
   571	          schema:
   572	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   573	          examples:
   574	            missing:
   575	              externalValue: openapi/examples/error-job-not-found.json
   576	    NodeNotFound:
   577	      description: NODE_NOT_FOUND (also for a malformed ID)
   578	      content:
   579	        application/json:
   580	          schema:
   581	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   582	    DownstreamFailure:
   583	      description: DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE
   584	      content:
   585	        application/json:
   586	          schema:
   587	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   588	    Unavailable:
   589	      description: CIRCUIT_OPEN (a downstream breaker is open) or OVERLOADED (request threads and queue full, every workflow slot busy, or a downstream call that could not start within its timeout; never counted against a breaker; SERVICES.md section 3)
   590	      content:
   591	        application/json:
   592	          schema:
   593	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   594	    Timeout:
   595	      description: DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT
   596	      content:
   597	        application/json:
   598	          schema:
   599	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   600	    Internal:
   601	      description: INTERNAL_ERROR (unexpected; traceId, no stack trace)
   602	      content:
   603	        application/json:
   604	          schema:
   605	            $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorBody'
   606	  schemas:
   607	    ErrorBody:
   608	      type: object
   609	      required: [error, message, traceId, requestId, retryable]
   610	      properties:
   611	        error:
   612	          type: string
   613	          description: scholarchain.core.ErrorCode (status and retryable are fixed per code)
   614	          enum: [MALFORMED_JSON, INVALID_FIELD, REQUEST_TOO_LARGE, UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN, ROUTE_NOT_FOUND, QUERY_NOT_FOUND, JOB_NOT_FOUND, NODE_NOT_FOUND, EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND, IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT, JOB_NOT_FINISHED, UNSUPPORTED_CONSTRAINT, UNSUPPORTED_RETRIEVAL_MODE, RATE_LIMITED, DOWNSTREAM_FAILURE, NOT_READY, CIRCUIT_OPEN, OVERLOADED, DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, DOWNSTREAM_TIMEOUT, INTERNAL_ERROR]
   615	        message:
   616	          type: string
   617	        traceId:
   618	          type: string
   619	        requestId:
   620	          type: string
   621	        retryable:
   622	          type: boolean
   623	    QueryBody:
   624	      type: object
   625	      required: [question]
   626	      additionalProperties: false
   627	      properties:
   628	        question:
   629	          type: string
   630	          description: 1 to runtime.question.max-chars characters after NFC normalization; no control characters but tab and newline
   631	        constraints:
   632	          type: object
   633	          additionalProperties: false
   634	          nullable: true
   635	          properties:
   636	            yearFrom: {type: integer, nullable: true}
   637	            yearTo: {type: integer, nullable: true}
   638	            topics: {type: array, items: {type: string}, nullable: true}
   639	            institutions: {type: array, items: {type: string}, nullable: true}
   640	            venues: {type: array, items: {type: string}, nullable: true}
   641	        analysis:
   642	          type: object
   643	          additionalProperties: false
   644	          nullable: true
   645	          properties:
   646	            rankingMetric:
   647	              type: string
   648	              nullable: true
   649	              enum: [PUBLICATION_COUNT, DISTINCT_COAUTHOR_COUNT, MAXIMUM_COAUTHORS_ON_ONE_PAPER, CROSS_COMMUNITY_DEGREE, COMMUNITIES_CONNECTED, PARTICIPATION_COEFFICIENT, CORENESS]
   650	            includeTeamSize: {type: boolean, nullable: true}
   651	            includeBridgeMetrics: {type: boolean, nullable: true}
   652	            includeCommunities: {type: boolean, nullable: true}
   653	            includePaths: {type: boolean, nullable: true}
   654	        retrieval:
   655	          type: object
   656	          additionalProperties: false
   657	          nullable: true
   658	          description: Each limit 1 to its configured maximum (retrieval.graph-limit, vector-limit, max-path-depth); above it is 422
   659	          properties:
   660	            graphLimit: {type: integer, nullable: true}
   661	            vectorLimit: {type: integer, nullable: true}
   662	            maxPathDepth: {type: integer, nullable: true}
   663	        output:
   664	          type: object
   665	          additionalProperties: false
   666	          nullable: true
   667	          description: Accepted for the spec contract; the answer always carries its claims' evidence IDs
   668	          properties:
   669	            includeEvidence: {type: boolean, default: true}
   670	            includeScores: {type: boolean, default: true}
   671	            includeUncertainty: {type: boolean, default: true}
   672	        execution:
   673	          type: string
   674	          enum: [sync, async]
   675	          default: sync
   676	    SyncQueryResponse:
   677	      type: object
   678	      required: [queryId, mode, answer, claims, links]
   679	      properties:
   680	        queryId: {type: string}
   681	        mode: {type: string, enum: [synchronous, asynchronous], description: asynchronous for GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/result}
   682	        answer:
   683	          type: object
   684	          required: [summary, abstained, abstention, uncertainty, window, resolutions, verification, answerHash]
   685	          description: abstention is the rag Abstention (reason, detail); window the rag WindowSelection; resolutions rag ResolutionSummary; verification the rag VerificationCounts (modules/rag/RUNTIME.md)
   686	        claims:
   687	          type: array
   688	          description: core AnswerClaim records (claimId, text, evidenceIds, verificationStatus, uncertainty, provenance)
   689	          items: {type: object}
   690	        links:
   691	          type: object
   692	          required: [explain, evidence]
   693	          properties:
   694	            explain: {type: string}
   695	            evidence: {type: array, items: {type: string}}
   696	    AsyncQueryResponse:
   697	      type: object
   698	      required: [jobId, queryId, mode, state, statusUrl]
   699	      properties:
   700	        jobId: {type: string, pattern: '^job-[0-9a-f]{32}$'}
   701	        queryId: {type: string, pattern: '^qry-[0-9a-f]{32}$'}
   702	        mode: {type: string, enum: [asynchronous]}
   703	        state: {$ref: '#/components/schemas/JobState'}
   704	        statusUrl: {type: string}
   705	    JobState:
   706	      type: string
   707	      enum: [QUEUED, RUNNING, SUCCEEDED, FAILED]
   708	      description: QUEUED -> RUNNING -> SUCCEEDED or FAILED; QUEUED -> FAILED for a job that never ran. SUCCEEDED and FAILED are final.
   709	    JobResponse:
   710	      type: object
   711	      required: [jobId, queryId, state, stage, createdAt, updatedAt, startedAt, finishedAt, error, statusUrl, resultUrl]
   712	      properties:
   713	        jobId: {type: string}
   714	        queryId: {type: string}
   715	        state: {$ref: '#/components/schemas/JobState'}
   716	        stage: {type: string, nullable: true, description: a workflow stage name}
   717	        createdAt: {type: string, format: date-time}
   718	        updatedAt: {type: string, format: date-time}
   719	        startedAt: {type: string, format: date-time, nullable: true}
   720	        finishedAt: {type: string, format: date-time, nullable: true}
   721	        error:
   722	          type: object
   723	          nullable: true
   724	          required: [error, message, retryable]
   725	          properties:
   726	            error: {type: string, description: an ErrorBody error code}
   727	            message: {type: string}
   728	            retryable: {type: boolean}
   729	        statusUrl: {type: string}
   730	        resultUrl: {type: string, nullable: true, description: present only when SUCCEEDED}
   731	    ExplainResponse:
   732	      type: object
   733	      description: Fields as in the example; nested records are the rag runtime records of the same names (modules/rag/RUNTIME.md)
   734	      required: [queryId, runId, createdAt, auditSchemaVersion, mode, llm, plan, resolutions, stages, templateIds, templateCalls, vectorIndexes, embeddingModelVersion, promptVersions, modelIds, graphEvidenceIds, semanticEvidenceIds, metricVersions, graphRunId, verification, removedClaims, droppedLines, abstained, abstentionReason, llmCalls, embeddingCalls, budget, counters, answerHash]
   735	      properties:
   736	        llm: {type: string, enum: [STUBBED, LIVE]}
   737	        mode: {type: string, enum: [FAKE, LIVE]}
   738	    EvidenceResponse:
   739	      type: object
   740	      required: [evidenceId, evidenceType, subjectIds, workId, sourceUri, chunkId, graphPath, score, text, textTruncated, textHash, facts, provenance]
   741	      properties:
   742	        evidenceId: {type: string}
   743	        evidenceType: {type: string}
   744	        subjectIds: {type: array, items: {type: string}}
   745	        workId: {type: string, nullable: true}
   746	        sourceUri: {type: string}
   747	        chunkId: {type: string, nullable: true}
   748	        graphPath: {type: array, items: {type: string}, nullable: true}
   749	        score: {type: number, nullable: true}
   750	        text: {type: string, nullable: true}
   751	        textTruncated: {type: boolean}
   752	        textHash: {type: string, nullable: true}
   753	        facts: {type: object, additionalProperties: {type: string}}
   754	        provenance: {type: object, description: core Provenance}
   755	    NodeView:
   756	      type: object
   757	      required: [nodeId, label, properties]
   758	      properties:
   759	        nodeId: {type: string}
   760	        label: {type: string}
   761	        properties: {type: object}
   762	    EdgeView:
   763	      type: object
   764	      required: [edgeId, edgeType, fromId, toId, properties]
   765	      properties:
   766	        edgeId: {type: string}
   767	        edgeType: {type: string}
   768	        fromId: {type: string}
   769	        toId: {type: string}
   770	        properties: {type: object}
   771	    NodeResponse:
   772	      type: object
   773	      required: [node]
   774	      properties:
   775	        node:
   776	          $ref: '#/components/schemas/NodeView'
   777	    NeighborsResponse:
   778	      type: object
   779	      required: [nodeId, direction, depth, limit, edgeTypes, nodes, edges, truncated, nextCursor]
   780	      properties:
   781	        nodeId: {type: string}
   782	        direction: {type: string, enum: [in, out, both]}
   783	        depth: {type: integer}
   784	        limit: {type: integer}
   785	        edgeTypes: {type: array, items: {type: string}}
   786	        nodes: {type: array, items: {$ref: '#/components/schemas/NodeView'}}
   787	        edges: {type: array, items: {$ref: '#/components/schemas/EdgeView'}}
   788	        truncated: {type: boolean}
   789	        nextCursor: {type: string, nullable: true}
   790	    Status:
   791	      type: object
   792	      required: [status]
   793	      properties:
   794	        status: {type: string, enum: [ok, ready]}
```

     1	{

     4	  "subjectIds": [
     5	    "scr:canon-v1.er-v2:3ed012c7e9d38ac0dc513403ec31176a"

     7	  "workId": null,
     8	  "sourceUri": "dynamodb:EntityLinkCache/name-v1:ada lovelace/scr:canon-v1.er-v2:3ed012c7e9d38ac0dc513403ec31176a",
     9	  "chunkId": null,
    10	  "graphPath": null,
    11	  "score": null,
